# Forms step 3e1, household and spouse: Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Ask Form 122A-1 line 1, the household list, the spouse's income and the county; store them encrypted; count the list the way each form defines its count; make current monthly income the filer's plus the spouse's; and print 122A-1's marital boxes and Column B, Schedule I's spouse column, Schedule J line 2 (with an attached sheet past five dependents) and Form 101's County.

**Architecture:** Two new one-to-one models on the session, `Household` (the county, the list as encrypted JSON, the next row id) and `SpouseIncome` (ten encrypted amounts), saved through the session PATCH as nested keys. Pure functions in `apps/intake/household.py` count the list per form, and `apps/eligibility/cmi.py` gains the household's CMI and one income-step check whose gaps Complete lists and the means test raises as `IncomeStepGap`. The forms read those functions through new derived rules, plus a `household.dependents[].<attr>` list binding for Schedule J line 2's repeat group. The wizard's step 1 gains County and loses joint filing, and its Income step asks, in this order, about marriage, the household, the filer's income and the spouse's income.

**Tech Stack:** Django 5.0 + DRF (Python 3.11), `encrypted-model-fields` (Fernet), PostgreSQL 15 / sqlite for tests, pypdf 6.13.1 and PyMuPDF 1.27.2.3; React 19 + Vite 7 + TypeScript, vitest + Testing Library + MSW, Playwright.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-24-forms-step3e1-design.md` (cited `§`), approved by the owner on 2026-10-24. It draws from the umbrella, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-forms-step3-design.md` (§5.5), and the persona targets, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-forms-step3-personas.md`. Where this plan and the spec disagree, the spec wins; say so in the ledger.

## Global Constraints

- **Encryption.**
  - `Household.county` is an `EncryptedCharField`, nullable.
  - `Household.members` is an `EncryptedTextField` holding the list as JSON, nullable.
  - `IncomeInfo.marital_status` is a nullable `EncryptedCharField` with no model `choices`.
  - The ten `SpouseIncome` amounts are `EncryptedDecimalField(max_digits=10, decimal_places=2, null=True, blank=True)` with no default.
  - A ciphertext test reads the raw column with SQL, since the library passes plaintext through without error.
- **Null is unanswered.**
  - `members` null means the filer never answered; `[]` means they said no one lives with them or depends on them.
  - `marital_status`, the county and every spouse box are null until answered.
  - A blank box is never $0 unless the filer typed 0.
- **No names are stored.** `relationship` is one of `spouse`, `son`, `daughter`, `stepchild`, `grandchild`, `parent`, `sibling`, `other_relative`, `unmarried_partner`, `roommate`, `other`, and is never free text.
- **The four marriage answers:** `not_married`, `married_spouse_filing`, `married_together`, `married_separately`. There is no fifth; a half-answered line 1 stores null.
- **The 18 counties, in this order and spelling:**
  - Eastern: `Cook`, `DuPage`, `Grundy`, `Kane`, `Kendall`, `Lake`, `LaSalle`, `Will`.
  - Western: `Boone`, `Carroll`, `DeKalb`, `Jo Daviess`, `Lee`, `McHenry`, `Ogle`, `Stephenson`, `Whiteside`, `Winnebago`.
- **Limits.**
  - Age is a whole number from 0 to 120.
  - The list holds at most 20 rows, at most one of them `spouse`.
  - `spouse_is_dependent` appears on the spouse's row only; a null on another row counts as absent.
  - Only those value checks give a 400. A missing answer saves, and Complete names it.
- **Row shape, as stored and returned:** `id`, `relationship`, `age`, `lives_with_you`, `claimed_or_supported`, then `spouse_is_dependent` on the spouse's row only; a missing answer is null. A wizard row adds a browser-only `_key`, and no key starting with `_` is ever sent.
- **Complete's new sentences (§9.1), verbatim:**
  - county: "Choose your county on the Your Information step."
  - marriage: "Answer the question about marriage on the Income step."
  - spouse filing: "DigniFi prepares forms for one person filing alone. Check your answer about marriage on the Income step."
  - list: "List the people in your home and the people you support on the Income step, or tell us no one does."
  - row: "Answer every question for each person on your household list on the Income step."
  - mismatch: "Your household list and your answer about marriage don't match. Check both on the Income step."
  - spouse income: "Enter your spouse's income by source on the Income step. If your spouse has no income, enter $0 in one box."
  - These stay as they are: "Enter your name and address on the Your Information step.", "Enter your 9-digit Social Security number on the Your Information step.", "Enter your income by source on the Income step.", "Go through the Expenses step, even if you have no expenses to enter.", "Check the debts read from your documents on the Amounts Owed step."
- **Other fixed words.**
  - §3.2's information: "DigniFi prepares forms for one person filing alone. It can't prepare a case your spouse files with you."
  - The glossary sentence: "A person who is economically dependent on you regardless of whether the person can be claimed as a dependent on your federal tax return."
  - "Is your spouse a dependent of yours?", "No one else lives with me or depends on me.", "Monthly income for the means test".
  - Line 1 and its declaration are shown in the form's words, with the column instructions left out and no "…" marks (rulings P2).
- **Where words live.**
  - DigniFi's own sentences go in `frontend/src/constants/copy.ts`, where `reading-level.test.ts` scores them. Line 13's draft strings carry a `// DRAFT: counsel C-3` comment; filers see no mark.
  - Text quoted from the forms goes in `frontend/src/constants/formText.ts`, which no test scores.
  - Refusal sentences are the server's.
  - No household or spouse value is ever passed to `trackEvent`.
- **The forms.** Every new or re-ruled placement gets its row in `data/forms/field_index/audit/step2_verification.json` in the same commit; every new derived text rule gets its `RULE_FIXES` entry; `backend/apps/forms/tests/known_defects.json` stays `[]`.
- **Tests** are named after outcomes, and use only synthetic data: SSNs from the 900 range, phone numbers 555-0100 to 555-0199. The backend tests' `_today` is pinned to 2026-10-16; never import `_today` by name.
- **Gates:** every task ends with backend pytest at coverage 90% or more, `ruff check backend/` (ruff 0.8.5), `makemigrations --check` when models change, the Postgres tests when the task touches one or a migration, and vitest, both `tsc -p … --noEmit` runs, eslint and prettier when it touches the frontend. The eight journeys run at the end of Tasks 6 and 11 (rulings P11).
- **The machine is shared with audio work.** Check `uptime` before a full suite and run it only when the 1-minute load is under 10. Run vitest with `--maxWorkers=2`.
- **Never run `tsc -b`.** It writes build output through the `node_modules` symlink into the main checkout.
- **November 1.** From 2026-11-01 the journeys, the seed and the forms calls that work the means test out refuse until the November median table is loaded (DIG-315). If that date has come and the table isn't loaded, stop at the journey gate and report. Never extend a table's `valid_through`.
- **Commits.** Set `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. Stage by explicit path only, never `git add -A`, `git add .` or `git commit -a`. Use `/usr/bin/git`, one plain command per call, and commit with `/usr/bin/git commit -F <file>`. A message ends with its last line about the change: **add no `Co-Authored-By`, `Claude-Session` or other agent trailer, even if your harness asks for one.** Never stage `.serena/`, `DESIGN.md`, `assets/`, `.impeccable` or `.claude/`.
- **No outward actions:** no push, PR, merge, deploy or production command. Those are the owner's. Nothing in 3e1 deploys.
- **Databases and keys:** never use the shared development database `dignifi_dev`; this plan makes its own. Never print, log or commit an API key; the journeys use a dummy key against a fake OpenAI server.

## Review Focus

Five inputs or conditions the spec implies that no other test exercises, most likely to bite first. Each has its test in the owning task.

1. **One PATCH that answers the whole Income step** must return `household.family_size` and `household.fee_waiver_household_size` worked out from that same PATCH, not from a related row cached before the write (Task 7, `test_a_patch_that_answers_the_income_step_returns_its_counts_in_the_reply`).
2. **A row changed away from spouse** must lose its `spouse_is_dependent` key in the wizard's body. Sending `false` or null on a non-spouse row would make every autosave a 400 (Task 6, "a row changed away from spouse sends no spouse question at all").
3. **Picking "Living in the same household" with 20 rows already** adds no spouse row, and Complete then names the mismatch (Task 6, "with 20 rows, picking living together adds no spouse row"; Task 7's Complete case for 20 non-spouse rows under `married_together`).
4. **A form marked filed before 3e1 with no stored values** downloads as an unfiled form does, so it refuses with 422 and `step` `income_info` (Task 7, `test_a_filed_form_with_no_stored_values_refuses_like_an_unfiled_one`).
5. **A session saved before 3e1** opens step 1 with its county shown as needed, so the filer sees why the step won't save or continue (Task 5, "a session with no county shows step 1's county as needed on load").

## Before you start (once per worktree)

- Work in `/Volumes/Containers/DigniFi/.claude/worktrees/forms-step3e1` (branch `feat/forms-step3e1`, which holds the spec and this plan). Every command block runs from that worktree's root.
- Backend tools come from the main checkout's virtualenv, and frontend tools from its `node_modules`. Pre-commit's eslint hook expects that symlink.
- The journeys and the Postgres tests need Colima running, with the `dignifi-db-1` Postgres container answering on host port 5433. The journeys also need nothing listening on ports 8000, 5173 or 4010.
- `pytest` uses `config.settings.test` (sqlite in memory). The Postgres tests use `config.settings.test_postgres`, which reads `DATABASE_URL`. `manage.py` and the journeys use the database below.

```bash
set -eo pipefail
mkdir -p backend/logs .claude   # the audit log handler needs backend/logs; CI creates it too
[ -e frontend/node_modules ] || ln -s /Volumes/Containers/DigniFi/frontend/node_modules frontend/node_modules
# CI's synthetic Fernet key (it encrypts nothing real), read from ci.yml rather than copied here
FERNET=$(sed -n 's/^ *FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY: //p' .github/workflows/ci.yml | head -1)
[ -n "$FERNET" ]
# A database of its own keeps the shared dev database on develop's migrations. The Postgres
# tests create and drop their own test database beside it.
docker exec dignifi-db-1 dropdb -U dignifi --if-exists dignifi_forms_step3e1
docker exec dignifi-db-1 createdb -U dignifi dignifi_forms_step3e1
cat > .claude/forms-step3e1.env <<ENV
# Settings for forms step 3e1 (git-ignored). Source from the worktree root.
export PY=/Volumes/Containers/DigniFi/backend/.venv/bin/python
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
export DATABASE_URL=postgres://dignifi:dignifi_dev_password@localhost:5433/dignifi_forms_step3e1
export DJANGO_SECRET_KEY=test-only-not-a-secret
export DEBUG=True
export ALLOWED_HOSTS=localhost,127.0.0.1
export CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:5173,http://127.0.0.1:5173
export FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY=$FERNET
export OCR_PROVIDER=openai
export OPENAI_API_KEY=e2e-dummy-key
export OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4010/v1
ENV
source .claude/forms-step3e1.env && $PY -c "import pypdf, pymupdf; assert pypdf.__version__ == '6.13.1', pypdf.__version__; assert pymupdf.__version__ == '1.27.2.3', pymupdf.__version__"
git check-ignore -q .claude/forms-step3e1.env && git check-ignore -q .claude/e2e.sh && echo "env file is ignored"
```

Expected: `env file is ignored`.

The journeys run through one script, which Tasks 6 and 11 call. Write it now:

```bash file=.claude/e2e.sh
#!/bin/zsh
# Run the Playwright journeys on this worktree's own database.
# usage (from the worktree root): zsh .claude/e2e.sh [runs=2]
# Migrates, loads the district data, reseeds the personas, starts runserver (:8000)
# and Vite (:5173), runs the journeys RUNS times, prints one summary line per run,
# and always stops both servers. Logs: .claude/e2e-logs/.
set -u
RUNS=${1:-2}
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)" || exit 2
source .claude/forms-step3e1.env
export DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.development PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1
LOG="$PWD/.claude/e2e-logs"
rm -rf "$LOG"; mkdir -p "$LOG"

for port in 8000 5173 4010; do
  if lsof -nP -iTCP:$port -sTCP:LISTEN >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "PORT $port BUSY: stop whatever holds it first"; exit 3
  fi
done

( cd backend && $PY manage.py migrate -v 0 && $PY manage.py loaddata ilnd_2025_data -v 0 \
  && $PY manage.py seed_demo_data --reset ) > "$LOG/setup.log" 2>&1 || { echo "SETUP FAILED"; tail -20 "$LOG/setup.log"; exit 4; }

( cd backend && exec $PY manage.py runserver 0.0.0.0:8000 --noreload ) > "$LOG/backend.log" 2>&1 &
BPID=$!
( cd frontend && exec ./node_modules/.bin/vite --port 5173 --strictPort ) > "$LOG/vite.log" 2>&1 &
FPID=$!
cleanup() { kill $BPID $FPID 2>/dev/null; wait $BPID $FPID 2>/dev/null; }
trap cleanup EXIT INT TERM

up() { $PY -c "import urllib.request,sys; urllib.request.urlopen(sys.argv[1], timeout=2)" "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }
for i in {1..60}; do up http://127.0.0.1:8000/health/ && up http://localhost:5173/ && break; sleep 1; done
up http://127.0.0.1:8000/health/ || { echo "BACKEND NOT UP"; tail -20 "$LOG/backend.log"; exit 5; }
up http://localhost:5173/ || { echo "VITE NOT UP"; tail -20 "$LOG/vite.log"; exit 5; }

overall=0
for r in $(seq 1 $RUNS); do
  ( cd frontend && npx playwright test --config e2e/playwright.config.ts --reporter=line ) > "$LOG/run$r.log" 2>&1
  rc=$?
  perl -pe 's/\e\[[0-9;]*[A-Za-z]//g' "$LOG/run$r.log" > "$LOG/run$r.txt"
  summary=$(grep -E "^\s*[0-9]+ (passed|failed|flaky|skipped|did not run)" "$LOG/run$r.txt" | tr -s ' ' | tr '\n' ' ' | sed 's/^ //')
  echo "RUN $r exit=$rc: $summary"
  if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
    overall=1
    grep -E "^\s+[0-9]+\) " "$LOG/run$r.txt" | head -10
  fi
done
exit $overall
```

It migrates the step's database, loads the district data, reseeds the personas, starts the backend on :8000 and Vite on :5173, runs every journey in `frontend/e2e/journeys/` `RUNS` times, prints one line per run, and always stops both servers. Its logs land in `.claude/e2e-logs/`. A failed run prints the failing journeys; read the trace under `frontend/test-results/` before running again, and don't retry blindly. Journeys are sensitive to machine load: under a load average far above the core count, Generate All can outrun the dashboard's wait. Check `uptime` before blaming the task.

Check the baseline before Task 1 (with the 1-minute load under 10):

```bash
set -eo pipefail
source .claude/forms-step3e1.env
(cd backend && $PY -m pytest --cov=apps --cov=config --cov-fail-under=90 --tb=short -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider 2>&1 | tail -6)
(cd backend && DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.development $PY manage.py check)
/Volumes/Containers/DigniFi/backend/.venv/bin/ruff check backend/
(cd frontend && NO_COLOR=1 npx vitest run --maxWorkers=2 2>&1 | grep -E '^ +(Test Files|Tests) ')
(cd frontend && npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit && npx tsc -p tsconfig.node.json --noEmit)
(cd frontend && npm run lint --silent && npm run format:check)
(cd backend && DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=config.settings.test_postgres $PY -m pytest -m postgres --tb=short -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider 2>&1 | tail -4)
```

Expected, from 3a2's final gates (develop `86bc32a` has the same tree plus one workflow file): `2836 passed, 25 skipped, 1 xfailed` with coverage above 90%, `System check identified no issues (0 silenced).`, `All checks passed!`, `Tests  423 passed (423)`, no output from tsc or eslint, `All matched files use Prettier code style!`, and `25 passed` for the Postgres tests. Record the counts you get in the ledger; each task's gate compares against them.

## Decisions already made

The spec's §16 lists the 22 calls the spec made, and the owner approved it. The plan adds these, each recorded with why and what it costs if wrong:

- **Task order.** Storage first (Tasks 1–3), then the seed and readback (4), then the two wizard steps and their journey page objects (5, 6), so the journeys answer the new questions before Task 7 makes Complete and the means test require them. Between Tasks 5 and 7 a wizard-created session sizes its means test at `DebtorInfo.household_size`'s default of 1, and until Task 11 the fee-waiver page pre-fills that 1. No test outcome changes and nothing deploys.
- **Line 1 on the step** shows the form's words with the column instructions left out, no "…" marks, and no trailing period on the four answers, as in the mockup the owner approved. The declaration is verbatim. `formText.ts` records each cut in a comment.
- **The shared builder** `answer_household` is created in Task 3 (`backend/apps/intake/tests/builders.py`), whose tests first need it: `answer_household(session, *, marital_status: str | None = "not_married", members: Sequence[dict] | None = (), county: str | None = "Cook", spouse_income: Mapping[str, Decimal | None] | None = None) -> Household`. It may be called again on the same session, works through the session's cached relations, and `spouse_income={}` stores an all-null row.
- **A mismatch is judged on answered data only.** A spouse row with `lives_with_you` unanswered is an unanswered row, not a mismatch. Under `married_together` with no answered spouse row, a row whose relationship is still unanswered suppresses the mismatch, since it may be the spouse; the row gap names it. So a half-answered list gives one sentence, not two.
- **The PATCH reply reads what that PATCH stored.** The session queryset caches its related rows (`select_related`), and `update_or_create` saves a separate object, so `update()` points the session at the `household`, `income_info` and `spouse_income` rows it saved (Tasks 1 and 2). No `refresh_from_db()`. The same staleness for `debtor_info` and `expense_info` predates 3e1 and is DIG-433, not fixed here.
- **`IncomeInfo.__str__`** stops printing the marriage answer in Task 2, which encrypts it, and drops `number_of_dependents` in Task 7: `f"IncomeInfo (Session: {self.session_id})"`.
- **Sheet columns and boxes (spec §10.3 change 2).** A sheet column may print a box slot's answer, as the field index's `option_text` of the state the answer maps to, when that box's `choices` gives every answer its own state (Schedule J's `{"yes": "/yes", "no": "/no"}`). A column is still refused when any box slot binding it has an empty map or a single-box map (`{"true": state}`), whose No would print blank, the same as no answer: `group {name!r}: sheet column {column.label!r} binds a box that doesn't give every answer its own state`. Task 9 loads `option_text` into `IndexWidget` and adds `field_index.option_text()`.
- **Schedule J line 2 on an unanswered list.** The household binding and `check2` pass `IncomeStepGap` through for a list never answered, rather than printing "No": the means test refuses first on every forms path (§9.2), and "No" would swear to an answer the filer never gave. Every Schedule J test session therefore gets a household (`[]` when the test needs no dependents).
- **The expense calculator** reads the household's CMI, except that with no income row `_get_cmi` still returns `Decimal("0")`, as its existing tests need. Its fallback to a family of 1 stays (DIG-152, 3h).
- **`household_size` and `filing_type`** leave the `DebtorInfo` serializer and the TS type in Task 11, with their last reader (`FeeWaiverPage`). The seed stops writing them then; the columns stay until step 5.
- **The county spelling pin.** Beyond the spec, Task 5 adds a backend test that the frontend's county list equals `ILND_COUNTIES`, since one drifted spelling would make every step 1 save a 400.
- **Names the spec doesn't give** (the owner can overturn any): the relationship words Spouse, Son, Daughter, Stepchild, Grandchild, Parent, Brother or sister, Other relative, Unmarried partner, Roommate, Other; rule names with a `_spouse` suffix, since a non-filing spouse is not Debtor 2; the Schedule J sheet's title (the template's printed form title followed by ", line 2", so its header reads "… line 2 (attached sheet)") and its column widths 220, 80 and 168 pt; `NOT_ANSWERED_YET`, `MARRIED_NOT_FILING`, `DEPENDENT_GLOSSARY_LEAD`; and Task 1's two server sentences for a second spouse row and a misplaced spouse question.

### Task 1: Household storage

The `Household` model, the county constant and the relationship words. The session read and PATCH nest `household`, with validation, ids and the reply in the order sent. The PATCH locks the session and reopens a completed intake. Nothing reads the household yet.

**Files:**

- Create:
  - `backend/apps/districts/counties.py`
  - `backend/apps/intake/household.py`
  - `backend/apps/intake/migrations/0016_household.py` (generated)
  - `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_household_storage.py`
- Modify:
  - `backend/apps/intake/models.py`: the import at `:9`. Insert `Household` after `IncomeInfo` (`:93-224`), before `ExpenseInfo` (`:227`).
  - `backend/apps/intake/serializers.py`:
    - the model import `:15-28`;
    - the new code after `_save_rows_by_id` (`:224-248`);
    - `_INTAKE_DATA` `:252`;
    - the nesting `:262-266`;
    - `Meta.fields` `:272-286`;
    - `create()` `:305-332`;
    - `update()` `:334-377`.
  - `backend/apps/intake/views.py:118`: `select_related`.
- Test:
  - `test_household_storage.py` (new);
  - `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_completion.py:328-337`: one more reopen parameter;
  - `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_session_lock.py:484-570`: one more `WRITES` entry (Postgres).

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
- Produces:
  - `apps.districts.counties.ILND_COUNTIES: dict[str, str]`: county → `"Eastern"` | `"Western"`, in §A's order and spelling.
  - `apps.intake.household`, pure, with no model imports:
    - `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS: dict[str, str]`;
    - `MAX_MEMBERS = 20`, `MAX_AGE = 120`;
    - `ANSWER_KEYS = ("relationship", "age", "lives_with_you", "claimed_or_supported")`;
    - `SPOUSE_KEY = "spouse_is_dependent"`.
  - `apps.intake.models.Household`:
    - `session = OneToOneField(IntakeSession, on_delete=CASCADE, related_name="household")`;
    - `county = EncryptedCharField(max_length=20, null=True, blank=True)`;
    - `members = EncryptedTextField(null=True, blank=True)`;
    - `next_member_id = PositiveIntegerField(default=1)`;
    - `created_at` (`auto_now_add`), `updated_at` (`auto_now`); `db_table = "household"`;
    - `get_members(self) -> list[dict] | None`;
    - `store_members(self, rows: list[dict] | None) -> list[dict] | None`, which doesn't save.
  - In `apps.intake.serializers`:
    - `HouseholdMemberSerializer` and `HouseholdSerializer`, as Step 5 gives them;
    - `_save_household(session: IntakeSession, data: dict) -> Household`;
    - `IntakeSessionSerializer.household = HouseholdSerializer(required=False, allow_null=True)`;
    - `_INTAKE_DATA` gains `"household"`.
  - The API:
    - `household.county` and `household.members`;
    - `household` is `null` for a session with no row;
    - a reply lists the rows in the order sent, each with its id.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:** none.

- No test pins the session read's key set or counts queries.
- The wizard hydrates only the sections it names (`frontend/src/pages/IntakeWizard.tsx:197-214`), so `household: null` changes nothing in the browser.

**Why the reply needs care.** `select_related("household")` caches the household on the session that DRF serializes after the save (`rest_framework/mixins.py:63-75`).

- A first save creates the row through `Household(session=…)`, which refreshes that cache.
- A later save's `get_or_create` returns a new object (`django/db/models/query.py:948`), so the reply would show the ids from before the PATCH. Task 6 pairs ids from that reply.

So `_save_household` points the session at the row it saved, and the tests read a second save's reply (P16).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_household_storage.py`:

```python
"""The household list and the county are stored encrypted, with stable ids (3e1 spec §4).

Business intent: each form counts the list its own way, so a row keeps exactly the answers the
filer gave. A missing answer saves, for Complete to name; only a value no answer can have is
refused. Ids are never reused, so the wizard can pair each row it sent with its id.
"""

from decimal import Decimal

import pytest
from django.db import connection
from rest_framework.test import APIClient

from apps.districts.counties import ILND_COUNTIES
from apps.districts.models import District
from apps.intake.models import Household, IntakeSession
from apps.users.models import User

ROW_KEYS = ["id", "relationship", "age", "lives_with_you", "claimed_or_supported"]
SON = {"relationship": "son", "age": 8, "lives_with_you": True, "claimed_or_supported": True}
DAUGHTER = {**SON, "relationship": "daughter", "age": 5}
ROOMMATE = {**SON, "relationship": "roommate", "age": 29, "claimed_or_supported": False}
UNANSWERED = dict.fromkeys(["age", "lives_with_you", "claimed_or_supported"])


@pytest.fixture
def filer(db):
    district = District.objects.create(
        code="ILND", name="Illinois Northern", state="IL",
        court_name="U.S. Bankruptcy Court ILND", filing_fee_chapter_7=Decimal("338.00"),
    )
    user = User.objects.create_user(username="filer", password="pw")
    client = APIClient()
    client.force_authenticate(user=user)
    return client, IntakeSession.objects.create(user=user, district=district)


def _url(session):
    return f"/api/intake/sessions/{session.pk}/"


def _save(client, session, household):
    return client.patch(_url(session), {"household": household}, format="json")


def _members(client, session, rows):
    """Save the list and return it as the reply gives it."""
    response = _save(client, session, {"members": rows})
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.data
    return response.data["household"]["members"]


def _ids(rows):
    return [row["id"] for row in rows]


def test_a_household_county_and_list_are_stored_as_ciphertext(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _save(client, session, {"county": "Cook", "members": [ROOMMATE]})
    household = Household.objects.get(session=session)
    with connection.cursor() as cursor:
        cursor.execute("SELECT county, members FROM household WHERE id = %s", [household.pk])
        county, members = cursor.fetchone()
    assert "Cook" not in county and "roommate" not in members
    assert household.county == "Cook"
    assert household.get_members()[0]["relationship"] == "roommate"


def test_each_new_row_gets_the_next_id_and_ids_are_never_reused(filer):
    client, session = filer
    first = _members(client, session, [SON, DAUGHTER, ROOMMATE])
    assert _ids(first) == [1, 2, 3]
    # The daughter is removed and a grandchild added: the new row gets 4, never 2.
    second = _members(client, session, [first[0], first[2], {"relationship": "grandchild"}])
    assert _ids(second) == [1, 3, 4]
    assert _ids(Household.objects.get(session=session).get_members()) == [1, 3, 4]


def test_a_sent_row_with_one_of_the_lists_ids_replaces_the_stored_row_whole(filer):
    client, session = filer
    [stored] = _members(client, session, [SON])
    # Unlike an asset or a debt row, an answer the row leaves out isn't kept.
    [replaced] = _members(client, session, [{"id": stored["id"], "relationship": "daughter"}])
    assert replaced == {"id": stored["id"], "relationship": "daughter", **UNANSWERED}


def test_a_row_with_an_id_the_list_doesnt_hold_gets_a_new_one(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _members(client, session, [SON, DAUGHTER, ROOMMATE])
    assert _ids(_members(client, session, [{**SON, "id": 99}])) == [4]


def test_an_id_sent_twice_keeps_it_only_once(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _members(client, session, [SON, DAUGHTER, ROOMMATE])
    twice = _members(client, session, [{**SON, "id": 1}, {**DAUGHTER, "id": 1}])
    assert [(r["id"], r["relationship"]) for r in twice] == [(1, "son"), (4, "daughter")]


def test_the_reply_lists_rows_in_the_order_sent_each_with_its_id(filer):
    client, session = filer
    son, daughter = _members(client, session, [SON, DAUGHTER])
    reply = _members(client, session, [daughter, ROOMMATE, son])
    assert [(r["id"], r["relationship"]) for r in reply] == [
        (2, "daughter"), (3, "roommate"), (1, "son"),
    ]
    assert client.get(_url(session)).data["household"]["members"] == reply


def test_only_the_spouses_row_carries_spouse_is_dependent(filer):
    client, session = filer
    spouse, son = _members(client, session, [
        {"relationship": "spouse", "age": 45, "spouse_is_dependent": False},
        {**SON, "spouse_is_dependent": None},  # a null on another row counts as absent
    ])
    assert list(spouse) == [*ROW_KEYS, "spouse_is_dependent"]
    assert spouse["spouse_is_dependent"] is False
    assert list(son) == ROW_KEYS


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "members",
    [
        [{"relationship": "cousin", "age": 30}],
        [{"relationship": "son", "age": -1}],
        [{"relationship": "parent", "age": 121}],
        [{"relationship": "son", "age": 7.5}],
        [{"relationship": "spouse", "age": 45}, {"relationship": "spouse", "age": 44}],
        [{**SON, "spouse_is_dependent": False}],
        [SON] * 21,
    ],
    ids=["unknown_relationship", "age_below_0", "age_above_120", "age_not_whole",
         "second_spouse", "spouse_question_on_a_son", "a_21st_row"],
)
def test_a_value_no_answer_can_have_is_refused(filer, members):
    client, session = filer
    IntakeSession.objects.filter(pk=session.pk).update(status="completed")

    response = _save(client, session, {"members": members})

    assert response.status_code == 400
    assert "members" in response.data["household"]
    assert IntakeSession.objects.get(pk=session.pk).status == "completed"  # nothing reopens
    assert not Household.objects.filter(session=session).exists()


def test_a_missing_answer_saves(filer):
    client, session = filer
    spouse, unknown = _members(client, session, [{"relationship": "spouse"}, {"age": 9}])
    assert spouse == {"id": 1, "relationship": "spouse", **UNANSWERED, "spouse_is_dependent": None}
    assert unknown == {"id": 2, "relationship": None, **UNANSWERED, "age": 9}
    assert list(spouse) == [*ROW_KEYS, "spouse_is_dependent"]
    assert list(unknown) == ROW_KEYS


def test_the_edges_of_each_limit_save(filer):
    client, session = filer
    edges = [{"relationship": "parent", "age": 120}, {"relationship": "son", "age": 0}]
    assert len(_members(client, session, edges + [ROOMMATE] * 18)) == 20


def test_a_county_outside_the_district_is_refused(filer):
    client, session = filer
    # The statute's spelling of a district county, not the one stored.
    assert _save(client, session, {"county": "Du Page"}).status_code == 400
    assert not Household.objects.filter(session=session).exists()
    assert _save(client, session, {"county": "DuPage"}).data["household"]["county"] == "DuPage"


def test_each_county_carries_its_division():
    # 3c prints the division on the List of Creditors' heading (3e1 spec §6, 28 U.S.C. § 93(a)).
    western = ["Boone", "Carroll", "DeKalb", "Jo Daviess", "Lee", "McHenry", "Ogle",
               "Stephenson", "Whiteside", "Winnebago"]
    eastern = ["Cook", "DuPage", "Grundy", "Kane", "Kendall", "Lake", "LaSalle", "Will"]
    assert sorted(c for c, d in ILND_COUNTIES.items() if d == "Western") == sorted(western)
    assert sorted(c for c, d in ILND_COUNTIES.items() if d == "Eastern") == sorted(eastern)
    assert len(ILND_COUNTIES) == 18  # no county outside the two divisions


def test_a_household_patch_updates_only_the_keys_sent(filer):
    client, session = filer
    assert client.get(_url(session)).data["household"] is None

    first = _save(client, session, {"county": "Cook"}).data["household"]
    assert (first["county"], first["members"]) == ("Cook", None)
    _members(client, session, [SON])
    kept = _save(client, session, {"county": "Kane"}).data["household"]

    assert kept["county"] == "Kane"
    assert [row["relationship"] for row in kept["members"]] == ["son"]


def test_a_new_session_can_carry_its_county(filer):
    client, session = filer
    body = {"district": session.district_id, "current_step": 1, "household": {"county": "Cook"}}
    created = client.post("/api/intake/sessions/", body, format="json")
    assert created.status_code == 201
    assert Household.objects.get(session_id=created.data["session"]["id"]).county == "Cook"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the two constant modules**

- **`backend/apps/districts/counties.py`:**
  - The docstring says these are the Northern District of Illinois's counties and divisions (28 U.S.C. § 93(a)). Membership is statutory, not a dated table, and no ZIP code decides it. Each county has one stored spelling, which Form 101 prints.
  - `ILND_COUNTIES` is a literal dict, one county per line:
    - `"Eastern"`: Cook, DuPage, Grundy, Kane, Kendall, Lake, LaSalle, Will;
    - `"Western"`: Boone, Carroll, DeKalb, Jo Daviess, Lee, McHenry, Ogle, Stephenson, Whiteside, Winnebago.
- **`backend/apps/intake/household.py`:**

  - The docstring says the list's words and limits (3e1 spec §4.2) live here, with no model imports.
  - The Interfaces constants, and `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS` as a literal dict, one entry per line, in this order:

    | Value               | Words               |
    | ------------------- | ------------------- |
    | `spouse`            | `Spouse`            |
    | `son`               | `Son`               |
    | `daughter`          | `Daughter`          |
    | `stepchild`         | `Stepchild`         |
    | `grandchild`        | `Grandchild`        |
    | `parent`            | `Parent`            |
    | `sibling`           | `Brother or sister` |
    | `other_relative`    | `Other relative`    |
    | `unmarried_partner` | `Unmarried partner` |
    | `roommate`          | `Roommate`          |
    | `other`             | `Other`             |

    Its comment says the list is never free text, so no name can be typed.

- [ ] **Step 4: Add `Household` and generate its migration**

In `models.py`, import `EncryptedTextField` beside `EncryptedCharField` (`:9`), `json`, and `ANSWER_KEYS, SPOUSE_KEY` from `.household`. Then add the class with the Interfaces fields:

- **Docstring:** the filer's county and the people in their home or whom they support, both encrypted (3e1 spec §4.1). `members` is JSON in the filer's order, so no encrypted column is ever sorted. Null means unanswered; `[]` means the filer said no one.
- **`__str__`** returns `f"Household (Session: {self.session_id})"`, never the county or a member.
- **`get_members()`** returns `json.loads(self.members)`, or None.
- **`store_members(rows)`** stores `json.dumps` of the rows it returns, or None for None.
  - Each row it returns holds `id`, then the four `ANSWER_KEYS`, then `SPOUSE_KEY` on the spouse's row only (`relationship == "spouse"`).
  - Any key the row didn't send is None.
- **The id rule:** a row keeps its `id` when the stored list holds it and no earlier row in this payload kept it. Every other row gets `next_member_id`, which then goes up by one. Storing null resets nothing.

```bash
source .claude/forms-step3e1.env
(cd backend && $PY manage.py makemigrations intake --name household)
```

Expected: `migrations/0016_household.py`, with `- Create model Household` alone.

- [ ] **Step 5: Nest the household in the session serializer**

In `serializers.py`, import `Household`, `ILND_COUNTIES`, and `MAX_AGE, MAX_MEMBERS, RELATIONSHIP_WORDS` from `.household`. Then add the following.

**`HouseholdMemberSerializer(serializers.Serializer)`:**

- `id = IntegerField(required=False)`.
- `relationship = ChoiceField(choices=list(RELATIONSHIP_WORDS), allow_null=True, required=False)`.
- `age = IntegerField(min_value=0, max_value=MAX_AGE, allow_null=True, required=False)`.
- `lives_with_you`, `claimed_or_supported` and `spouse_is_dependent`, each `BooleanField(allow_null=True, required=False)`.

**`HouseholdSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer)`:**

- `county = ChoiceField(choices=list(ILND_COUNTIES), allow_null=True, required=False)`.
- `members = ListField(child=HouseholdMemberSerializer(), allow_null=True, required=False, max_length=MAX_MEMBERS, write_only=True)`.
- `Meta`: `model = Household`, `fields = ["county", "members"]`.
- **`validate_members`** passes None through. It raises `ValidationError` in two cases, in the plan's own words (P21):
  - more than one `spouse` row: "Only one person on the list can be your spouse.";
  - a non-null `spouse_is_dependent` on any other row: "Only your spouse's row answers whether your spouse is a dependent."
- **`to_representation`** sets `data["members"] = instance.get_members()` on `super()`'s dict.
  - `write_only` keeps `ListField` from iterating the stored JSON string by character, and keeps the child from adding `spouse_is_dependent: null` to every row.
  - Task 7 adds its counts as `SerializerMethodField`s.

**`_save_household(session, data) -> Household`**, beside `_save_rows_by_id`:

1. `get_or_create(session=session)`.
2. Set `county` if `"county"` was sent.
3. Run `store_members(data["members"])` if `"members"` was sent.
4. `save()`.
5. Set `session.household = household`, so the reply reads this row rather than the cached copy.
6. Return the household.

**The session serializer:**

- `_INTAKE_DATA` gains `"household"`.
- `IntakeSessionSerializer` declares `household` after `debts`, and `Meta.fields` lists it after `"debts"`.
- `create()` pops `household` before `IntakeSession.objects.create`, then runs `if household_data: _save_household(session, household_data)`.
- `update()` pops it before the `setattr` loop, saves it the same way after the expense save, and names it in its docstring.
  - As with `debtor_info`, `null` and `{}` write nothing.
  - Either one still reopens a completed intake (`:342-343`).

In `views.py:118`, add `"household"` to `select_related`.

```text
feat(intake): store the household list and county, encrypted

Household, one-to-one with the session, holds the filer's county and
the people in their home or whom they support (3e1 spec §4). Both are
encrypted. The list is JSON in the filer's order, so no encrypted
column is ever sorted. Null means unanswered, and [] means the filer
said no one.

The session read and PATCH nest household, and a PATCH sets only the
keys it sends. A row sent with one of the list's ids keeps it and
replaces the stored row whole. Any other row, including one whose id
an earlier row in the payload already kept, gets next_member_id, so
ids are never reused. The reply lists the rows in the order sent.

A missing answer saves. Only a value no answer can have is a 400: an
unknown relationship, an age outside 0-120 or not whole, a second
spouse row, the spouse question on another row, a 21st row, or a
county outside the Northern District's 18.

The PATCH already locks the session, and now reopens a completed
intake when it carries household. Its reply reads the household the
save wrote, not the copy select_related cached before it, so a second
save's ids come back too.

Nothing reads the household yet.
```

The four line 1 answers, encrypted, through a hand-written migration whose reverse is tested on sqlite and Postgres. `SpouseIncome`, its serializer and its nesting. The old marital values move in the tests and the seed. Nothing reads the answer or the spouse's amounts yet.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:**

- **`apps/forms/tests/test_api_readback.py`** breaks. It PATCHes each seed persona's `income` (`:102-104`), and the seed's `"single"` and `"married_joint"` are now a 400. The fix is the seed's six values (Step 6).

  - the 47 lines;

- **Left for Task 7:** 13 lines, because the means test's fallback still reads `married_joint` and `married_separate`. They are listed in Step 6.
- **No persona's figures move.** The fallback runs only without a `DebtorInfo.household_size` (`eligibility/models.py:122-128`, `expense_deduction_calculator.py:70-72`), and every persona has one. No derivation reads `marital_status`.

**The reply.** As in Task 1, `select_related` caches `income_info` and `spouse_income`, and `update_or_create` returns new objects (`django/db/models/query.py:986`). So `update()` points the session at the rows it saved, and Task 7's counts read `marital_status` through that session (P16).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_spouse_and_marriage_storage.py`. Copy in the `filer` fixture from `test_household_storage.py`, which Task 1 committed: an ILND `District`, a `User`, an authenticated `APIClient`, and a new `IntakeSession`, returned as `(client, session)`. Then add:

```python
"""The marriage answer and the spouse's income are stored encrypted (3e1 spec §3.3, §5.2).

Business intent: Form 122A-1 line 1 is a sworn answer in the form's words, so the server takes
only its four answers, never an old tax-filing status. The spouse's amounts are new data,
encrypted from the start and bounded as the filer's are; a blank box stays blank.
"""

from decimal import Decimal

import pytest
from django.db import connection
from rest_framework.test import APIClient

from apps.districts.models import District
from apps.intake.models import IncomeInfo, IntakeSession, SpouseIncome
from apps.users.models import User

TEN_EMPTY = dict.fromkeys(IncomeInfo.SOURCE_FIELDS)


def _save(client, session, body):
    return client.patch(f"/api/intake/sessions/{session.pk}/", body, format="json")


def _raw(sql, session):
    with connection.cursor() as cursor:
        cursor.execute(sql, [session.pk])
        return cursor.fetchone()[0]


def test_a_marriage_answer_is_stored_as_ciphertext(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _save(client, session, {"income_info": {"marital_status": "married_together"}})
    raw = _raw("SELECT marital_status FROM income_info WHERE session_id = %s", session)
    assert "married_together" not in raw
    assert IncomeInfo.objects.get(session=session).marital_status == "married_together"


def test_a_spouse_amount_is_stored_as_ciphertext(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _save(client, session, {"spouse_income": {"monthly_gross_wages": "1800.00"}})
    raw = _raw("SELECT monthly_gross_wages FROM spouse_income WHERE session_id = %s", session)
    assert "1800" not in raw
    assert SpouseIncome.objects.get(session=session).monthly_gross_wages == Decimal("1800.00")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("answer", ["married_joint", "single", ""])
def test_an_old_tax_filing_status_is_refused(filer, answer):
    client, session = filer
    response = _save(client, session, {"income_info": {"marital_status": answer}})
    assert response.status_code == 400
    assert "marital_status" in response.data["income_info"]
    assert not IncomeInfo.objects.filter(session=session).exists()


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "amount", ["-1.00", "100000000.00", "NaN", "Infinity", "12.345"],
    ids=["negative", "too_wide", "nan", "infinite", "fraction_of_a_cent"],
)
def test_a_spouse_amount_is_bounded_as_the_filers(filer, amount):
    client, session = filer
    assert _save(client, session, {"spouse_income": {"monthly_pension": amount}}).status_code == 400
    assert not SpouseIncome.objects.filter(session=session).exists()


def test_a_blank_spouse_box_is_null_not_zero(filer):
    client, session = filer
    sent = {**TEN_EMPTY, "monthly_gross_wages": "1000.00", "monthly_tips": "0"}
    reply = _save(client, session, {"spouse_income": sent}).data["spouse_income"]
    spouse = SpouseIncome.objects.get(session=session)
    assert (spouse.monthly_overtime, spouse.monthly_tips) == (None, Decimal("0.00"))
    assert (reply["monthly_overtime"], reply["monthly_tips"]) == (None, "0.00")


def test_a_changed_marriage_answer_and_spouse_amount_come_back_in_the_reply(filer):
    # The second save updates stored rows: its reply shows them as saved, not as read before.
    client, session = filer
    _save(client, session, {"income_info": {"marital_status": "married_together"},
                            "spouse_income": {"monthly_gross_wages": "1000.00"}})
    reply = _save(client, session, {"income_info": {"marital_status": "married_separately"},
                                    "spouse_income": {"monthly_gross_wages": "1200.00"}}).data
    assert reply["income_info"]["marital_status"] == "married_separately"
    assert reply["spouse_income"]["monthly_gross_wages"] == "1200.00"


def test_an_income_rows_str_never_shows_its_marriage_answer(filer):
    # __str__ reaches the admin and the logs, where the encrypted answer must not appear (P17).
    _, session = filer
    income = IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, marital_status="married_separately")
    assert "married" not in str(income)


def test_a_new_session_can_carry_its_spouse_income(filer):
    client, session = filer
    body = {"district": session.district_id, "current_step": 1,
            "spouse_income": {"monthly_gross_wages": "1000.00"}}
    created = client.post("/api/intake/sessions/", body, format="json")
    assert created.status_code == 201
    stored = SpouseIncome.objects.get(session_id=created.data["session"]["id"])
    assert stored.monthly_gross_wages == Decimal("1000.00")
```

```python
def _stored_marriage(apps):
    """The one income row's raw marital_status column, and its wages as the model reads them."""
    with connection.cursor() as cursor:
        cursor.execute("SELECT marital_status FROM income_info")
        [(raw,)] = cursor.fetchall()
    return raw, apps.get_model("intake", "IncomeInfo").objects.get().monthly_gross_wages


def _run_and_undo_the_marriage_migration(migrate):
    """An old tax status saved before 0017: the row after 0017, then after undoing it."""
    apps = migrate(HOUSEHOLD)
    apps.get_model("intake", "IncomeInfo").objects.create(
        session_id=_session(apps).pk,
        marital_status="married_joint",
        monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("3000.00"),
    )
    return _stored_marriage(migrate(MARRIAGE_ENCRYPTED)), _stored_marriage(migrate(HOUSEHOLD))


def test_undoing_the_marriage_migration_restores_an_empty_column(migrate):
    # No tax status is a line 1 answer, so none carries over. Undone, the old column comes
    # back on a table with rows, empty in each, and the amounts are untouched both ways.
    after, undone = _run_and_undo_the_marriage_migration(migrate)
    assert after == (None, Decimal("3000.00"))
    assert undone == ("", Decimal("3000.00"))


@pytest.mark.postgres
@pytest.mark.skipif(
    connection.vendor != "postgresql",
    reason="needs Postgres: the undo on a real column type; CI runs it with config.settings.test_postgres",
)
def test_undoing_the_marriage_migration_restores_an_empty_column_on_postgres(migrate):
    after, undone = _run_and_undo_the_marriage_migration(migrate)
    assert (after, undone) == ((None, Decimal("3000.00")), ("", Decimal("3000.00")))
    with connection.cursor() as cursor:
        cursor.execute(
            "SELECT data_type, character_maximum_length FROM information_schema.columns"
            " WHERE table_name = 'income_info' AND column_name = 'marital_status'"
        )
        assert cursor.fetchone() == ("character varying", 30)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write migration 0017 by hand, before touching the model**

The spec fixes the three operations (§3.3). The last AddField must equal what the model's field deconstructs to, and the library drops `max_length` (`encrypted_model_fields/fields.py:93-99`).

```python file=backend/apps/intake/migrations/0017_incomeinfo_marital_status_encrypted.py
"""Store the Income step's marriage answer encrypted, as Form 122A-1 line 1 asks it (3e1 spec §3.3).

None of the old tax-filing values is a line 1 answer, so none carries over: the old column is
dropped and an empty encrypted one added. Written by hand: makemigrations would alter the
column in place, keeping the plaintext, and its reverse would put ciphertext in a
30-character column. The AlterField gives the old column a default of "", so undoing the
RemoveField can add it back to a table with rows: undone, every row holds "".
"""

import encrypted_model_fields.fields
from django.db import migrations, models


class Migration(migrations.Migration):

    dependencies = [
        ("intake", "0016_household"),
    ]

    operations = [
        migrations.AlterField(
            model_name="incomeinfo",
            name="marital_status",
            field=models.CharField(
                blank=True,
                choices=[
                    ("single", "Single"),
                    ("married_joint", "Married Filing Jointly"),
                    ("married_separate", "Married Filing Separately"),
                ],
                default="",
                max_length=30,
            ),
        ),
        migrations.RemoveField(
            model_name="incomeinfo",
            name="marital_status",
        ),
        migrations.AddField(
            model_name="incomeinfo",
            name="marital_status",
            field=encrypted_model_fields.fields.EncryptedCharField(blank=True, null=True),
        ),
    ]
```

In `models.py`:

- **`MARITAL_ANSWERS`** goes after `SOURCE_FIELDS`. Its comment: Form 122A-1 line 1's four answers (3e1 spec §3.2); null means unanswered, and there is no fifth.
- **`marital_status`** replaces `:113-120` with the Interfaces field. Its comment: line 1's answer, encrypted; the serializer takes only `MARITAL_ANSWERS`.
- **`IncomeInfo.__str__`** (`:207-211`) drops the answer, so no plaintext reaches the admin or a log (P17): `f"IncomeInfo: {self.number_of_dependents} dependents (Session: {self.session_id})"`. Task 7 drops the dependents.
- **`SpouseIncome`** goes after `Household`.
  - Its docstring: the spouse's monthly income by the filer's ten sources, for a filer who lives with their spouse (3e1 spec §5.2), encrypted from the start, and kept when the marriage answer changes.

```text
feat(intake): encrypt the marriage answer, store the spouse's income

IncomeInfo.marital_status now holds Form 122A-1 line 1's answer:
not_married, married_spouse_filing, married_together or
married_separately, or null while unanswered. It is encrypted
(3e1 spec §3), and anything else, the old tax-filing statuses and ""
included, is a 400. IncomeInfo's __str__ no longer prints it.

None of the old values is a line 1 answer, so none carries over.
Migration 0017 is written by hand. It gives the old column a default
of "", drops it and adds the encrypted one; makemigrations would have
kept the plaintext. Undone, the old column comes back holding "" in
every row, on sqlite and on Postgres.

SpouseIncome holds the spouse's ten amounts by the filer's sources.
They are encrypted, bounded as the filer's are, and null when blank.
The session read and PATCH nest it, and saving it reopens a completed
intake. Nothing counts it yet. A PATCH's reply now shows the income
and spouse rows it saved, not the copies read before the save.

The seed and the tests move to the new answers: Priya and DeShawn
live with their spouses, and the other personas aren't married. The
means test's married_joint and married_separate cases stay until it
stops reading them.
```

### Task 3: Counts, the household's CMI and the income-step gaps

This task adds pure functions, the household's CMI and the one income-step check, each with unit tests. Nothing in the app calls them yet; Task 7 does. It also writes the shared test builder, `answer_household`, because these tests are the first to need it (P14).

**Files:**

- Modify: `backend/apps/intake/household.py` (Task 1's module, kept free of model imports): add the five count functions after Task 1's constants.
- Modify: `backend/apps/eligibility/cmi.py`:
  - imports after `:11`;
  - the new class, sentences and functions after `current_monthly_income` (`:26-45`), before `is_above_median` (`:48`).
- Create: `backend/apps/intake/tests/builders.py`. The folder has no `__init__.py`, so it imports as `apps.intake.tests.builders`, a module of the namespace package `apps.intake.tests`, as `apps.forms.tests.oracle` is (`test_known_defects.py:14`).
- Test: `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_household_counts.py` (new, no database).
- Test: `backend/apps/eligibility/tests/test_household_cmi.py` (new).

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes:
  - From Task 1:
    - `ANSWER_KEYS` and `SPOUSE_KEY` (`apps.intake.household`);
    - `Household` (`related_name="household"`, with `county`);
    - `Household.get_members(self) -> list[dict] | None`;
    - `Household.store_members(self, rows: list[dict] | None) -> list[dict] | None`, which doesn't save.
  - From Task 2:
    - `IncomeInfo.marital_status`, which is nullable and has no model `choices`, so the ORM stores any string;
    - `IncomeInfo.MARITAL_ANSWERS`;
    - `SpouseIncome` (`related_name="spouse_income"`, with `SOURCE_FIELDS`).
  - Already in the code:
    - `IncomeNotSplit`, whose `__init__` takes no message (`cmi.py:14-23`);
    - `current_monthly_income(income_info) -> Decimal` (`:26-45`).
- Produces, in `apps.intake.household`. Each reads keys with `.get`, so rows without `id`, or without `spouse_is_dependent`, work.
  - `is_answered(member: dict) -> bool`: every `ANSWER_KEYS` value is not None, and so is `SPOUSE_KEY` on a `"spouse"` row. `0` and `False` count as answers.
  - `count_family_size(members: list[dict]) -> int`: 1, plus each row with `lives_with_you is True`. This is 122A-1 line 13 and the median.
  - `count_exemptions(members: list[dict]) -> int`: 1, plus each row with `claimed_or_supported is True`. This is 122A-2 line 5.
  - `dependents(members: list[dict]) -> list[dict]`: the rows themselves, in list order. It takes each non-spouse row with `claimed_or_supported is True`, and the spouse's row only when `spouse_is_dependent is True`. This is Schedule J line 2.
  - `count_fee_waiver_household(members: list[dict], marital_status: str | None) -> int`. This is 103B line 1, and it counts:
    - 1 for the filer;
    - plus 1 for `married_spouse_filing` or `married_together`;
    - plus `len(dependents(members))`, without the spouse's row when the spouse was already counted.
- Produces, in `apps.eligibility.cmi`:
  - `class IncomeStepGap(IncomeNotSplit)`. Its `__init__(self, message: str) -> None` calls `ValueError.__init__(self, message)`. It inherits `step`.
  - `MARRIAGE_UNANSWERED`, `SPOUSE_FILING`, `HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED`, `MEMBER_UNANSWERED`, `HOUSEHOLD_MISMATCH` and `SPOUSE_INCOME_NOT_SPLIT`: contract §A's sentences, verbatim.
  - `counted_spouse_income(session) -> SpouseIncome | None`: the session's spouse row when `income_info.marital_status == "married_together"`, and otherwise None, never raising. **It is also None under `married_together` with no row stored**, so a caller that reads an amount from it checks for None first.
  - `spouse_monthly_income(session) -> Decimal | None`:
    - None unless the answer is `married_together`. It reads the answer itself, never whether the row exists.
    - It raises `IncomeStepGap(SPOUSE_INCOME_NOT_SPLIT)` when there is no row, or all ten amounts are None.
    - Otherwise it returns `current_monthly_income(row)`.
  - `household_cmi(session) -> Decimal`: `current_monthly_income(session.income_info) + (spouse_monthly_income(session) or 0)`. With no income row it raises `IncomeNotSplit()`. It works out the filer's column first, and passes any inner refusal on unchanged.
  - `household_members(session) -> list[dict]`: the stored list. When there is no `Household` row, or the list is null, it raises `IncomeStepGap(HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED)`.
  - `income_step_gaps(session) -> list[IncomeStepGap]`, which never raises (Step 5).
- Produces, in `apps.intake.tests.builders`:
  - `answer_household(session, *, marital_status: str | None = "not_married", members: Sequence[dict] | None = (), county: str | None = "Cook", spouse_income: Mapping[str, Decimal | None] | None = None) -> Household` (Step 6, P14). It needs an income row, and may be called again on the same session.
  - Task 4 calls it in every shared fixture, and Tasks 7–10 call it in their tests.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:** none, because nothing outside these tests calls the new names. `cmi.py`'s import of `apps.intake.models` makes no cycle:

- `apps.intake` loads before `apps.eligibility` (`config/settings/base.py:66`, `:68`).
- `apps/intake/models.py:3-11` imports nothing from eligibility.
- `derivations.py:19` already imports a model for its annotations.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the count tests**

`test_household_counts.py` needs no database. Its docstring states the intent: each form counts the list by its own definition (§4.4), so a spouse or a roommate counts on one form and not another.

```python
def _row(relationship, age, lives_with_you, claimed_or_supported, **spouse):
    return {"relationship": relationship, "age": age, "lives_with_you": lives_with_you,
            "claimed_or_supported": claimed_or_supported, **spouse}

# The persona targets' lists (§11); each spouse answered No to "Is your spouse a dependent of yours?" (3e1.Q1).
MARIA = [_row("son", 8, True, True), _row("daughter", 5, True, True)]
JAMES = []
PRIYA = [_row("spouse", 45, True, True, spouse_is_dependent=False),
         _row("daughter", 10, True, True), _row("son", 7, True, True)]
DESHAWN = [_row("spouse", 44, True, True, spouse_is_dependent=False)]
SARAH = [_row("roommate", 29, True, False)]
ANDRES = [_row("daughter", 9, True, True)]
```

The tests and their assertions:

- **`test_line_13_counts_the_filer_and_everyone_at_home`** (six cases): `count_family_size` gives MARIA 3, JAMES 1, PRIYA 4, DESHAWN 2, SARAH 2 and ANDRES 2.
- **`test_a_roommate_counts_in_line_13_but_not_as_a_dependent`**: for SARAH, `count_family_size` is 2, `dependents` is `[]` and `count_exemptions` is 1.
- **`test_a_spouse_on_a_joint_tax_return_isnt_a_dependent_unless_the_filer_says_so`**:
  - `dependents(PRIYA) == [PRIYA[1], PRIYA[2]]`;
  - `dependents(DESHAWN) == []`;
  - with `spouse = {**DESHAWN[0], "spouse_is_dependent": True}`, `dependents([spouse]) == [spouse]`.
- **`test_line_5_counts_everyone_claimed_or_supported`**: `count_exemptions(PRIYA) == 4`.
- **`test_103b_counts_a_spouse_at_home_once`** (four cases):
  - parametrize over `marital_status` in `married_together` and `married_spouse_filing`, and over `spouse_is_dependent` False and True;
  - DESHAWN with that `spouse_is_dependent` gives 2;
  - `count_fee_waiver_household(MARIA, "not_married") == 3`;
  - `count_fee_waiver_household(SARAH, "not_married") == 1`.
- **`test_103b_counts_a_separated_spouse_only_as_a_dependent`** (two cases): `[_row("spouse", 44, False, True, spouse_is_dependent=d)]` under `married_separately` gives 2 when `d` is True and 1 when it is False.
- **`test_a_row_is_answered_only_when_every_question_is`** (eight cases), each asserting `is_answered(row) is answered`:

  - True: `_row("son", 8, True, True)`, `_row("son", 0, False, False)` and `_row("spouse", 45, True, True, spouse_is_dependent=False)`;
  - False: `_row("son", None, True, True)`, `_row(None, 8, True, True)`, `_row("son", 8, True, None)`, `_row("spouse", 45, True, True)` and `_row("spouse", 45, True, True, spouse_is_dependent=None)`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the CMI, gap and builder tests**

`test_household_cmi.py` follows `test_cmi.py`: a docstring with the business intent, and the sentences as literals, so a typo in a constant fails a test.

```python
MARRIAGE = "Answer the question about marriage on the Income step."
SPOUSE_FILING = "DigniFi prepares forms for one person filing alone. Check your answer about marriage on the Income step."
LIST = "List the people in your home and the people you support on the Income step, or tell us no one does."
ROW = "Answer every question for each person on your household list on the Income step."
MISMATCH = "Your household list and your answer about marriage don't match. Check both on the Income step."
INCOME_BY_SOURCE = "Enter your income by source on the Income step."
SPOUSE_INCOME = "Enter your spouse's income by source on the Income step. If your spouse has no income, enter $0 in one box."
SPOUSE_AT_HOME = {"relationship": "spouse", "age": 45, "lives_with_you": True,
                  "claimed_or_supported": True, "spouse_is_dependent": False}
SON = {"relationship": "son", "age": 7, "lives_with_you": True, "claimed_or_supported": True}

@pytest.fixture
def session(db):
    call_command("loaddata", "ilnd_2025_data", verbosity=0)
    user = User.objects.create_user(username="filer", password="pw")
    return IntakeSession.objects.create(user=user, district=District.objects.get(code="ilnd"))

def _answered(session, wages="12000.00", **answers):
    IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, monthly_gross_wages=Decimal(wages))
    return answer_household(session, **answers)

def _spouse(wages):  # as the personas type it: wages, and $0 in the other nine boxes
    return {**dict.fromkeys(SpouseIncome.SOURCE_FIELDS, Decimal("0.00")), "monthly_gross_wages": Decimal(wages)}

def _messages(session):
    return [str(gap) for gap in income_step_gaps(session)]

@pytest.mark.parametrize("wages, spouse, cmi", [("12000.00", "1800.00", "13800.00"), ("3500.00", "1000.00", "4500.00")],
                         ids=["priya", "deshawn"])
def test_a_filer_living_with_their_spouse_is_compared_on_both_incomes(session, wages, spouse, cmi):
    _answered(session, wages, marital_status="married_together", members=[SPOUSE_AT_HOME], spouse_income=_spouse(spouse))
    fresh = IntakeSession.objects.get(pk=session.pk)  # read back through the encrypted columns
    assert spouse_monthly_income(fresh) == Decimal(spouse)
    assert household_cmi(fresh) == Decimal(cmi)
    assert income_step_gaps(fresh) == []
```

The remaining CMI tests:

The gap tests:

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the five count functions to `household.py`**

Use the signatures and rules in Interfaces. Each docstring names the form line it counts.

- [ ] **Step 5: Add the new names to `cmi.py`**

Import `is_answered` from `apps.intake.household`, and `IncomeInfo` and `SpouseIncome` from `apps.intake.models`. Read each relation as `getattr(session, "<name>", None)`: Django's missing reverse one-to-one is an `AttributeError`, so this gives None.

Leave the module docstring (`:1-9`) alone. Its "the one figure … share" stays true until Task 7 moves the readers over.

`income_step_gaps` is the one algorithm the signatures don't settle. Let `answer = getattr(getattr(session, "income_info", None), "marital_status", None)`. A null `members`, or no `Household` row, means "no list". Check the rules in this order, adding at most one gap for each:

1. **Marriage.** `answer not in IncomeInfo.MARITAL_ANSWERS`, which covers None and any old stored value, gives `MARRIAGE_UNANSWERED`.
2. **Spouse filing.** `answer == "married_spouse_filing"` gives `SPOUSE_FILING`.
3. **List.** No list gives `HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED`.
4. **Row.** Any row with `not is_answered(row)` gives `MEMBER_UNANSWERED`, once.
5. **Mismatch.** This is judged only when the answer is one of the four and there is a list. "The spouse row" is the row whose `relationship == "spouse"`, and there is at most one.
   - Under `married_together` with a spouse row, its `lives_with_you is False` is a mismatch.
   - Under `married_together` with no spouse row, it is a mismatch unless some row has `relationship` None and `lives_with_you is not False`.
   - Under `not_married`, any spouse row is a mismatch.
   - Under the other two answers, nothing is a mismatch.
6. **Income.** No income row, or `IncomeNotSplit` from `current_monthly_income`, gives `IncomeStepGap(str(IncomeNotSplit()))`.
7. **Spouse's income.** The `IncomeStepGap` that `spouse_monthly_income` raises, if it raises one.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write `builders.py`**

The module docstring says the values are synthetic, and that the defaults make a session that passes 3e1's new checks: not married, `[]` ("no one"), and Cook. `answer_household`:

- **Marriage.** It sets `session.income_info.marital_status` and saves that field. With no income row, the access raises `IncomeInfo.DoesNotExist`, and the builder lets it raise. It doesn't check the answer; an unknown value shows up as the marriage gap.
- **Household row.** It works through the session's own relations, never a fresh query: `getattr(session, "household", None)` or `Household(session=session)`, and the same for `spouse_income`. A test holding `session` therefore reads what was stored. A second call replaces the first call's answer, county and list, so Task 7 can change one answer on a fixture.
- **List.** It stores `store_members(None if members is None else [dict(row) for row in members])`, sets `county`, and saves. The copies keep a caller's module-level rows from gaining an id.
- **Spouse's amounts.** When `spouse_income is not None`, an empty mapping included, it sets all ten fields, with a missing key stored as None. It saves, then calls `refresh_from_db()`, so the row holds the Decimals the encrypted column returns (eligibility facts F5). When `spouse_income` is None, it leaves any stored row alone.
- **Return.** It returns the household.

```text
feat(intake): household counts, a spouse's CMI and the income-step gaps

Each form counts the household list by its own definition: 122A-1
line 13 everyone at home, 122A-2 line 5 everyone claimed or supported,
Schedule J line 2 the dependents (a spouse only when the filer says so),
and 103B line 1 a spouse at home once. The household's current monthly
income adds the spouse's ten amounts for a filer living with them.

income_step_gaps names every gap the Income step can leave, in order,

on what the filer has answered. Nothing calls these yet. The shared test
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the seed tests**

Add to `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_seed_demo_data.py`, importing `Decimal`, `FeeWaiverApplication`, `IntakeSession` and `SpouseIncome` (`apps.intake.models`) and `PERSONAS` (`apps.intake.management.commands.seed_demo_data`, as `test_api_readback.py:31` does) beside `:8-14`:

```python
def _row(id, relationship, age, lives_with_you, claimed_or_supported, **spouse):
    return {"id": id, "relationship": relationship, "age": age, "lives_with_you": lives_with_you,
            "claimed_or_supported": claimed_or_supported, **spouse}

@pytest.mark.django_db
@pytest.mark.parametrize("persona, marital_status, members, spouse_wages, county", [
    ("maria", "not_married", [_row(1, "son", 8, True, True), _row(2, "daughter", 5, True, True)], None, "Cook"),
    ("james", "not_married", [], None, "Cook"),
    ("priya", "married_together", [_row(1, "spouse", 45, True, True, spouse_is_dependent=False),
                                   _row(2, "daughter", 10, True, True), _row(3, "son", 7, True, True)], "1800.00", "Cook"),
    ("deshawn", "married_together", [_row(1, "spouse", 44, True, True, spouse_is_dependent=False)], "1000.00", "Cook"),
    ("sarah", "not_married", [_row(1, "roommate", 29, True, False)], None, "Cook"),
    ("andres", "not_married", [_row(1, "daughter", 9, True, True)], None, "Kane"),
])
def test_the_seed_gives_each_persona_their_household_spouse_income_and_county(
    persona, marital_status, members, spouse_wages, county
):
    call_command("loaddata", "ilnd_2025_data", verbosity=0)
    call_command("seed_demo_data", persona=persona, stdout=StringIO())
    session = IntakeSession.objects.get(user__username=f"demo_{persona}")

    assert session.income_info.marital_status == marital_status
    assert session.household.county == county
    assert session.household.get_members() == members
    if spouse_wages is None:
        assert not SpouseIncome.objects.filter(session=session).exists()
    else:  # the spouse's wages, and $0.00 typed in the other nine boxes (§11)
        assert {f: getattr(session.spouse_income, f) for f in SpouseIncome.SOURCE_FIELDS} == {
            **dict.fromkeys(SpouseIncome.SOURCE_FIELDS, Decimal("0.00")), "monthly_gross_wages": Decimal(spouse_wages)}

@pytest.mark.django_db
def test_the_seed_gives_the_fee_waiver_application_103b_line_1s_count(monkeypatch):
    call_command("loaddata", "ilnd_2025_data", verbosity=0)
    for persona in ("maria", "deshawn", "sarah"):
        # Step 1's old Household Size box says 9, so only the household list can give 3, 2 and 1.
        data = PERSONAS[persona]()
        data["debtor"]["household_size"] = 9
        monkeypatch.setitem(PERSONAS, persona, lambda data=data: data)
        call_command("seed_demo_data", persona=persona, stdout=StringIO())

    assert {fw.session.user.username: fw.household_size for fw in FeeWaiverApplication.objects.all()} == {
        "demo_maria": 3, "demo_deshawn": 2, "demo_sarah": 1}
```

At size 9 the in-force April 2026 row's median is 182,302.00 + 11,100.00. All three personas stay well under 60% of it, so each still gets an application.

### Task 5: Step 1 asks the county, and stops asking household size and joint filing

Step 1 gains a required County select (§6) and loses the "Household & Filing Information" section (§7, §8.1). Its body becomes `{debtor_info, household: {county}}`. The frontend types take every 3e1 shape now, except that `DebtorInfo.household_size` leaves with its last reader in Task 11 (P6). The E2E page object picks each persona's county. Line numbers are at 85dc976, and this is the first task to touch these frontend files.

**Files:**

- Create:
  - `frontend/src/constants/counties.ts`
  - `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_household_frontend_copy.py`
- Modify:
  - `frontend/src/types/api.ts`:
    - `IntakeSession` `:25-43`;
    - in `DebtorInfo`, drop `filing_type` (`:66`) and the co-debtor block with its comment (`:68-73`), and keep `household_size` (`:64-65`);
    - `IncomeInfo` `:76-99`;
    - new types after `INCOME_FIELDS` (`:106-117`).
  - `DebtorInfoStep.tsx`:
    - props `:17-21`, signature `:36-40`;
    - drop `FILING_TYPES` `:31-34`;
    - `errors`: replace `:127-133`, and add `county` to the deps at `:136`;
    - County after the City/State/ZIP row, which closes at `:285`;
    - drop `:317-407`.
  - `IntakeWizard.tsx`:
    - state after `:120`;
    - hydration `:197`;
    - body `:248-249`, plus `county` in the deps at `:288`, which the contract leaves out;
    - render `:456-463`.
  - `CodebtorsStep.tsx:84-85`.
  - `copy.ts`, after `:123`.
  - `test/fixtures/session.ts:44-49`. The contract says `:45-49`, but `filing_type` is `:44`.
  - `frontend/e2e/fixtures/personas.ts`:
    - `PersonaDebtor` `:12-28`;
    - the `household_size`/`filing_type` pairs at `:105-106`, `:180-181`, `:253-254`, `:321-322`, `:412-413`, `:473-474`;
    - `PersonaFeeWaiver` `:83-87`;
    - `fee_waiver` `:165`, `:395`, `:456`.
  - `frontend/e2e/pages/wizard.page.ts:110-112`.
  - `maria-happy-path.spec.ts:81`, `deshawn-assets.spec.ts:81`, `sarah-simplest.spec.ts:80`.
- Test:
  - `DebtorInfoStep.test.tsx`;
  - `CodebtorsStep.test.tsx`, which the contract leaves out;
  - `IntakeWizard.test.tsx`, at `:79-117` and a new describe;
  - `test_household_frontend_copy.py`.

**Interfaces:**

- **Consumes:**
  - From Task 1, the API key `household.county`: a session PATCH takes `{"household": {"county": "<name>"}}`, and the read returns the name or `null`.
  - From Task 1, `apps.districts.counties.ILND_COUNTIES: dict[str, str]`, in §A order.
- **Produces, in `types/api.ts`:**
  - `MaritalStatus`, the four answers;
  - `Relationship`, the 11 §A values;
  - `interface HouseholdMember { id?: number; relationship: Relationship | null; age: number | null; lives_with_you: boolean | null; claimed_or_supported: boolean | null; spouse_is_dependent?: boolean | null }`;
  - `interface Household { county: string | null; members: HouseholdMember[] | null; readonly family_size?: number | null; readonly fee_waiver_household_size?: number | null }`;
  - `type SpouseIncome = Partial<Record<(typeof INCOME_FIELDS)[number], number | null>>`;
  - `IntakeSession` gains `household?: Household | null` and `spouse_income?: SpouseIncome | null`;
  - `IncomeInfo` gains `marital_status?: MaritalStatus | null`;
  - `DebtorInfo` loses `filing_type` and `co_debtor_*`.
- **Produces, `counties.ts`:** `export const ILND_COUNTIES = [`, then one `'<name>',` per line in §A order (`'Jo Daviess'` is two words), then `] as const;`.
- **Produces, `copy.ts`:** under a `// Your Information Step` banner, `COUNTY_OUTSIDE_DISTRICT = 'DigniFi prepares forms only for the Northern District of Illinois. Its 18 counties are the ones in this list.'`. This is information that never says where to file (§6, [UPL]). The reading-level formula scores it grade 6.9.
- **Produces, `DebtorInfoStep`:**
  - New props `county: string | null; onCountyChange: (county: string | null) => void`. The wizard holds the county, because it saves under `household`.
  - `<FormSelect label="County" name="county" required options={ILND_COUNTIES.map((name) => ({ value: name, label: name }))} helpText={COUNTY_OUTSIDE_DISTRICT} />`. Its error is "Please choose your county", inline like its siblings'. The blank option sends `null`.
- **Produces, the wizard:** `const [county, setCounty] = useState<string | null>(null)`, hydrated by `if (fresh.household) setCounty(fresh.household.county)`. The step 1 body is `{ debtor_info: debtorData, household: { county } }`.
- **Produces, E2E:**
  - `PersonaDebtor` gains `county: string` and loses `household_size` and `filing_type`;
  - `PersonaFeeWaiver` gains `household_size: number`: MARIA 3, DESHAWN 2 and SARAH 1, the §4.4 103B line 1 counts;
  - `fillDebtorInfo` runs `select[name="county"]` `.selectOption(data.county)`.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:**

- **`fillDebtorInfoFields`.** It is at `IntakeWizard.test.tsx:79-117`, with callers at `:618`, `:642`, `:672`, `:701` and `:1729`. It fills the Household Size and Filing Type controls (`:111-116`), so `getByRole` throws, and Continue now needs a county. Fix: drop those fills and pick a county (Step 1).
- **`tsc` on `test/fixtures/session.ts:44-49`.** `filing_type` and `co_debtor_*` are excess properties now. Fix: delete them. `household_size` (`:43`) stays until Task 11.
- **`DebtorInfoStep.test.tsx:15` and `:26-30`** pass `county={null} onCountyChange={noop}`. Test files are outside `tsconfig.app.json`, so nothing else would flag them.
- **E2E.** Every journey's step 1 fills two boxes that are gone and has no county. Three journeys read `X.debtor.household_size`. Fix: Step 8. Nothing type-checks `frontend/e2e`: `tsconfig.node.json` includes only `vite.config.ts`, and Playwright doesn't check types. A missed reference would type `"undefined"`, so Step 8 ends with a grep. The journeys next run at the end of Task 6 (P11).
- **Backend:** nothing breaks.
- **Intermediate figure (contract §D).** Until Task 7, a wizard-made session keeps `household_size`'s default of 1, and until Task 11 the fee-waiver page pre-fills it. Every persona's routing holds at the HH1 median of 73,180.00.

```text
feat(intake): ask the county on step 1, and drop joint filing

Form 101 prints the county, so step 1 now asks every filer to pick one
of the Northern District of Illinois's 18 counties. It saves beside the
debtor's answers, under household, and the step neither saves nor
continues without it. A filer who lives elsewhere is told which district
DigniFi prepares forms for.

DigniFi prepares forms for one person filing alone, so Household Size,
Filing Type and the spouse's identity boxes come off the step, and no
spouse SSN leaves the browser. The Codebtors step stops promising that a
joint filer's spouse appears.

The frontend types take the household, the spouse's income and the
marriage answer. A backend test pins the county list to the server's,
and the journeys pick each persona's county.
```
