### Task 6: The Income step asks the family first: marriage, the household, then both incomes

Order (§8.2): line 1 in the form's words (§3.2), the household list (§4.2), today's ten boxes, the spouse's ten for `married_together` only (§5.1), then the totals. The body becomes `{income_info: {the ten, marital_status}, household: {members}, spouse_income?}`. The wizard holds the rows, as it holds asset rows, so saved ids reach them. Lines are at 85dc976. Where Task 5 moved one, find it by the code quoted.

**Files:**

- **Create:**
  - `frontend/src/constants/household.ts`
  - `frontend/src/constants/formText.ts`
  - `frontend/src/utils/householdRows.ts`. The helpers can't live in a component file under eslint's `react-refresh/only-export-components`.
  - `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/HouseholdList.tsx` (P21).
- **Modify:**
  - `IncomeInfoStep.tsx`: props `:17-21`, state `:30`, errors `:45-59`, emit `:62-72`, sections before `:120`, spouse boxes after `:277`, totals `:279-294`.
  - `IntakeWizard.tsx`: state, `listSavesRef` `:106`, hydration `:197-199`, body `:251-259` and deps `:288`, `saveCurrentStepData` `:419-447`, render `:465-472`. The contract says `:461-470`.
  - `MeansTestPreview.tsx:110`, `:114`.
  - `copy.ts`.
  - `e2e/fixtures/personas.ts`: `:43-48`, the six `income` blocks (`:112-116`, `:187-191`, `:260-266`, `:328-332`, `:419-423`, `:480-497`), and the comments `:263-264`, `:396-397`.
  - `e2e/pages/wizard.page.ts:117-124`.
  - Comments `priya-ineligible.spec.ts:3-5`, `:34` and `deshawn-assets.spec.ts:39`, `:78-79`. The plan adds these.
- **Test:**
  - `IncomeInfoStep.test.tsx`: `:31-47` and 13 tests.
  - `IntakeWizard.test.tsx`: `:451-453`, `:1141-1201`, `:1547-1555`, `:1619`, `:1630-1632`, and 11 tests.
  - `MeansTestPreview.test.tsx`: 1 test.
  - `test_household_frontend_copy.py`: 1 test.

**Interfaces:**

- **Consumes:**
  - **Task 5:** its types, and the wizard's `county` with its `if (fresh.household)` block.
  - **Task 1:** `household.members`. Null means unanswered and `[]` means no one. The reply lists the rows in the order sent, each with its `id`, in §A's shape. Also `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS` (py).
  - **Task 2:** `income_info.marital_status`, which is the four answers or null, and `spouse_income`, the ten as decimal strings or null.
- **`household.ts`:**
  - `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS: Record<Relationship, string>`, one `  <value>: '<words>',` per line, in §A order, with P8's words: Spouse, Son, Daughter, Stepchild, Grandchild, Parent, Brother or sister, Other relative, Unmarried partner, Roommate, Other.
  - `MAX_HOUSEHOLD_ROWS = 20`
  - `MAX_AGE = 120`
- **`formText.ts`.** No test scores it. Its file comment says the step drops each answer's column instruction. Each constant's comment names its cut, taken from `b_122a-1.pdf` page 1. No "…" (P2), and the four answers drop the period where a cut ends them (P22); the declaration keeps its punctuation.

  - `LINE_1_QUESTION = 'What is your marital and filing status? Check one only.'`
  - `MARRIED_NOT_FILING = 'Married and your spouse is NOT filing with you. You and your spouse are:'`. The plan adds this: the third answer has no stored value.
  - `MARRIAGE_ANSWER_WORDS: Record<MaritalStatus, string>`:

    - `not_married`: 'Not married'
    - `married_spouse_filing`: 'Married and your spouse is filing with you'
    - `married_together`: 'Living in the same household and are not legally separated'
    - `married_separately`: 'Living separately or are legally separated'

    Cuts: ". Fill out Column A, lines 2-11." for the first; ". Fill out both Columns A and B, lines 2-11." for the second and third; ". Fill out Column A, lines 2-11; do not fill out Column B." for the fourth.

  - `LIVING_SEPARATELY_DECLARATION = 'By checking this box, you declare under penalty of perjury that you and your spouse are legally separated under nonbankruptcy law that applies or that you and your spouse are living apart for reasons that do not include evading the Means Test requirements. 11 U.S.C. § 707(b)(7)(B).'`
  - `DEPENDENT_GLOSSARY = 'A person who is economically dependent on you regardless of whether the person can be claimed as a dependent on your federal tax return.'`

- **`copy.ts`, `// Income Step` banner.** Exact values, each grade 7 or below by the test's formula:
  - `SPOUSE_FILING_INFO`: "DigniFi prepares forms for one person filing alone. It can't prepare a case your spouse files with you."
  - `HOUSEHOLD_NO_NAMES`: "List everyone who lives with you, and anyone you support who lives somewhere else. Don't include yourself. Don't type anyone's name."
  - `LIVES_WITH_YOU_HELP`: "Answer Yes if this person lives in your home with you." Marked `// DRAFT: counsel C-3`.
  - `NO_ONE_ELSE`: "No one else lives with me or depends on me."
  - `SPOUSE_DEPENDENT_QUESTION`: "Is your spouse a dependent of yours?"
  - `DEPENDENT_GLOSSARY_LEAD`: "The court forms' instructions define a dependent as:". Plan-added.
  - `AGE_OUT_OF_RANGE`: "Please enter an age from 0 to 120, in whole years"
  - `NOT_ANSWERED_YET`: "Not answered yet". Plan-added; Task 11 reuses it (P21).
  - `SPOUSE_INCOME_HELP`: "Enter your spouse's average monthly income before taxes. Leave a box blank if your spouse doesn't get that kind of income. If your spouse has no income at all, enter $0 in any box."
  - Under the preview banner:
    - `MEANS_TEST_INCOME_LABEL`: "Monthly income for the means test"
    - `MEDIAN_FOR_HOUSEHOLD_OF`: "Median for Household of". Marked `// DRAFT: counsel C-3`.
- **`utils/householdRows.ts`:**
  - `newHouseholdMember(answers: Partial<HouseholdMember> = {}): WizardRow<HouseholdMember>` returns `{ _key: 'new-<n>', relationship: null, age: null, lives_with_you: null, claimed_or_supported: null, ...answers }`. `<n>` comes from a module-level counter, never `crypto.randomUUID()`.
  - `isTouched(row): boolean` is true when the row has an `id`, or any of the four answers is non-null. It is read off the row and never stored, so the row keeps §A's shape.
  - `sentMember(row)` rebuilds a row in §A order: `_key`; then `id`, if any; then the four answers, each `?? null`; then `spouse_is_dependent ?? null`, only when the row is the spouse. Bodies and saved copies both pass through it. `withSavedIds` appends `id` last, and `useAutoSave` compares copies with `JSON.stringify`, so without the rebuild the next body would differ and save again.
  - `membersToSend(rows, noOneElse: boolean): WizardRow<HouseholdMember>[] | null` returns the touched rows, in order, through `sentMember`. With none, it returns `[]` when `noOneElse` is true and `null` otherwise.
- **`HouseholdList` props:** `rows`, `onRowsChange(rows)`, `noOneElse`, `onNoOneElseChange(pressed)`, `ageErrors: Record<number, string>`, `followedKey: string | null`, `onFollowedKeyChange(key)`.
- **`IncomeInfoStep`, new props:**
  - `householdRows` and `onHouseholdRowsChange`, controlled;
  - `noOneElse` and `onNoOneElseChange`;
  - `initialSpouseIncome?: SpouseIncome` and `onSpouseIncomeChange(data)`, which the step copies.
- **Wizard state:**
  - `householdRows = (fresh.household.members ?? []).map((m) => ({ _key: String(m.id), ...m }))`;
  - `noOneElse`, true when the list loaded as `[]`;
  - `spouseIncomeData`, set only `if (fresh.spouse_income)`, through `hydrateAmounts(…, INCOME_FIELDS)`;
  - `listSavesRef = useRef({ assets: 0, debts: 0, members: 0 })`.
- **Locators** (E2E and tests):
  - **Line 1:** radios `marital_status` with values `not_married`, `married_spouse_filing` and `not_filing`. `not_filing` is browser-only. Under it, radios `spouse_household` with `married_together` and `married_separately`.
  - **Rows:** `relationship_<i>` (a select) and `age_<i>`; yes/no radios `lives_with_you_<i>` and `claimed_or_supported_<i>`, and on the spouse's row also `spouse_is_dependent_<i>`, each pair with `value="yes"` and `value="no"` (`FeeWaiverPage.tsx`'s radios carry no `value`, and `pick` and the journeys select by it).
  - **Buttons:** `Remove person <i+1>`, "+ Add a person", and the `NO_ONE_ELSE` button with `aria-pressed`.
  - **Spouse boxes:** `spouse_<field>`, labelled "Spouse's " plus the filer box's label.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:**

All of these are fixed in Step 1 or Step 2:

- **`IncomeInfoStep.test.tsx`, every test.** The emit effect calls the missing `onSpouseIncomeChange` and throws a `TypeError`. Fix: `renderStep` gets a stateful harness.
- **`IntakeWizard.test.tsx:1619`, `:1630-1632`.** Their bodies now carry the new keys. Fix: expect `{ income_info: { ...savedAmounts, marital_status: null }, household: { members: null } }`.
- **`:1554`, `marital_status: ''`.** It would be sent and refused (Task 2). Fix: `null`. Drop `:1555` and reword `:1547-1549`.
- **`completedSession` `:451-453`, which echoes `'single'`.** Fix: `'not_married'`, with no `number_of_dependents`, plus `household: { county: 'Cook', members: [] }`.
- **`serveSessionSaves` `:1161`.** It stores only assets and debts. Fix: it also stores a sent `household.members`. A null list stays null. Otherwise each row keeps an id it holds or gets `nextId++`, and the reply's `household` is the one stored.
- **E2E and the backend:** nothing breaks, because an unanswered question never blocks. The page object answers anyway, before Task 7 requires it.

`renderStep(initialData, { rows = [], noOneElse = false, spouse } = {})` renders a harness that keeps `rows` and `noOneElse` in `useState` and spies on `onHouseholdRowsChange`. It returns `lastEmitted`, `canContinue`, `lastRows()` and `onRowsChange`.

Helpers:

- `field(name)`, as at `IntakeWizard.test.tsx:1204`;
- `pick(name, value)`, which clicks `input[name][value]`;
- `person(id, relationship, extra)`, a loaded row with `age: 30`, `true`/`true`.

The tests assert the spec's words as literals.

1. **"nothing is selected on line 1 until the filer picks".** The three `marital_status` radios (`not_married`, `married_spouse_filing`, `not_filing`) are present, no radio is checked, `queryByLabelText('Living in the same household and are not legally separated')` is null, and `lastEmitted().marital_status ?? null` is null.
2. **"the spouse filing answer says DigniFi prepares forms for one person".** Pick `married_spouse_filing`. `getByText("DigniFi prepares forms for one person filing alone. It can't prepare a case your spouse files with you.")` is present, the answer is `'married_spouse_filing'`, and `canContinue()` is true, since Complete names it.
3. **"picking Living in the same household adds a spouse row that lives with you".** Pick `not_filing`, then `married_together`.
   - `lastRows()` holds one row that `toMatchObject({ _key: expect.stringMatching(/^new-\d+$/), relationship: 'spouse', age: null, lives_with_you: true, claimed_or_supported: null })`.
   - Pick `married_separately`: `lives_with_you` is null.
   - Pick `married_together`: still one row, and true.
   - Pick `lives_with_you_0`=`no`, then `married_separately`: it stays false.
4. **"with 20 rows, picking living together adds no spouse row"** (P10 #3). Start with 20 `person(i, 'other')` rows. After the picks there are still 20, and none is `spouse`.
5. **"a loaded spouse row's answers are never changed by the marriage answer".** Start with `person(1, 'spouse', { lives_with_you: false, spouse_is_dependent: false })`. Pick `not_filing`, `married_together`, then `married_separately`. `onRowsChange` is never called.
6. **"Spouse is offered only when no other row is the spouse".** Start with a spouse row and a son. `relationship_0`'s options include `spouse`, and `relationship_1`'s don't.

9. **"an age outside 0–120 or not whole blocks saving".**
   - For `'-1'`, `'121'` and `'7.5'`: `age_0`'s accessible description is 'Please enter an age from 0 to 120, in whole years', and `canContinue()` is false.
   - For `'0'` and `'120'`: `canContinue()` is true.

    - Type `'12.345'` in `spouse_monthly_tips`: `canContinue()` is false.
    - Pick `married_separately`: the box is gone, and `canContinue()` is true.
11. **"the Income step asks the family first, in the form's words"** (3e1.Q3, §3.2, §4.2). Start with `marital_status: 'married_together'` and `person(1, 'spouse', { spouse_is_dependent: false })`.
    - A group named 'What is your marital and filing status? Check one only.' is present, and `getByText` finds the declaration whole: 'By checking this box, you declare under penalty of perjury that you and your spouse are legally separated under nonbankruptcy law that applies or that you and your spouse are living apart for reasons that do not include evading the Means Test requirements. 11 U.S.C. § 707(b)(7)(B).'
    - In document order (`compareDocumentPosition` gives `DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING` for each next one): the heading 'Your marriage', the heading 'People in your home, and people you support', the `monthly_gross_wages` box, the heading "Your spouse's income", the `spouse_monthly_gross_wages` box.
    - "List everyone who lives with you, and anyone you support who lives somewhere else. Don't include yourself. Don't type anyone's name." is shown.
    - Within the spouse's card, 'Is your spouse a dependent of yours?' and the glossary sentence ('A person who is economically dependent on you regardless of whether the person can be claimed as a dependent on your federal tax return.', matched as a substring) are both shown.
12. **"answering anything on an added row clears No one else"** (§8.2). Start with `noOneElse: true`. Click "+ Add a person" and set `relationship_0` to `son`: the 'No one else lives with me or depends on me.' toggle is gone. Click "Remove person 1": the toggle shows again, with `aria-pressed="false"`.

Start with filer wages `'12000.00'`, `marital_status: 'married_together'` and `spouse: { monthly_gross_wages: '1800.00' }`. The total labelled 'Your income' reads `$12,000.00` and the one labelled "Your spouse's income" `$1,800.00`. After picking `not_married`, no "Your spouse's income" total is shown.

Today's regexes (`/monthly wages/i`, `/social security/i`) would match a spouse box, but no existing test shows the spouse section. New tests find those boxes by `name`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Build `HouseholdList`**

- **Layout.** Headed "People in your home, and people you support", then `HOUSEHOLD_NO_NAMES` and `LIVES_WITH_YOU_HELP`.
- **One card per row, keyed by `row._key`** (never the index, so radios stay with their row on Remove). Each card has:
  - a Relationship select, without `spouse` when another row is the spouse. Blank is `null`.
  - an Age box, `number`, 0–120, step 1. Blank is `null`, never `Number('')`, which is 0.
  - "Lives with you?" and "Claimed on your tax return, or supported by you?" as yes/no `fieldset`s, in the markup of `FeeWaiverPage.tsx:205-228`. Null shows nothing checked.
  - on the spouse's row, `SPOUSE_DEPENDENT_QUESTION`, then `{DEPENDENT_GLOSSARY_LEAD} “{DEPENDENT_GLOSSARY}”`.
  - `NOT_ANSWERED_YET` beside each null answer on a touched row.
  - a Remove button.
- **Below the cards.** "+ Add a person", disabled at `MAX_HOUSEHOLD_ROWS`, and the `NO_ONE_ELSE` toggle, shown only while no row is touched.
- **Every answer** calls `onRowsChange` and `onNoOneElseChange(false)`.
- **The spouse key.** Becoming spouse sets `spouse_is_dependent: null`. Leaving spouse **deletes** the key, never `false` (P10 #2).
- **The followed row.** Answering `lives_with_you` on it, or removing it, calls `onFollowedKeyChange(null)`.
- **Never from an effect.** Nothing calls `onRowsChange` from an effect or on mount, since that would save on every visit.

- [ ] **Step 6: Rework `IncomeInfoStep`, family first**

- **State.**
  - `pick: 'not_married' | 'married_spouse_filing' | 'not_filing' | null`, from `initialData.marital_status`. Either sub-answer maps to `not_filing`.
  - `followedKey`, null, for this visit only.
  - `spouseData`, from `initialSpouseIncome || {}`.
- **Line 1.** A `fieldset` legended `LINE_1_QUESTION`.
  - Under `not_filing`, a nested `fieldset` holds the two sub-answers. `LIVING_SEPARATELY_DECLARATION` shows in full beside "Living separately", and `NOT_ANSWERED_YET` shows until one is picked.
  - Under `married_spouse_filing`, an `info-box` with `SPOUSE_FILING_INFO`.
  - `not_filing` keeps a sub-answer already given, or else sets `marital_status` to `null`. Every other pick stores its own value.
- **On each change of the answer**, in the same handler:
  - To `married_together`, when there is no spouse row and fewer than 20 rows: `onHouseholdRowsChange([...rows, newHouseholdMember({ relationship: 'spouse', lives_with_you: true, spouse_is_dependent: null })])`, then set `followedKey` to its key and call `onNoOneElseChange(false)`.
  - Otherwise, while the `followedKey` row is still the spouse, set its `lives_with_you` to `true` under `married_together` and to `null` under any other answer. A loaded row is never followed (U:308).
- **Sections, in order.**
  1. The explainer and the split notice.
  2. "Your marriage".
  3. `HouseholdList`.
  4. Today's three income sections, unchanged.
  5. "Your spouse's income", for `married_together` only: `SPOUSE_INCOME_HELP`, then the ten `spouse_<field>` boxes built like the filer's.
  6. The totals.
- **Errors.**
  - The filer's ten.
  - `spouse_<field>` by `amountError(…, 10)`, under `married_together` only.
  - `age_<i>`: `AGE_OUT_OF_RANGE` unless the age is null or a whole number from 0 to `MAX_AGE`.
  - An unanswered question is never an error.
- **The emit effect** also calls `onSpouseIncomeChange(spouseData)`.
- **Totals.** "Your income" and, under `married_together`, "Your spouse's income". Each amount sits in its own `total-amount` element, so `getByText('$3,200.00')` (`:95`) holds. "Total Monthly Income" goes.

- [ ] **Step 7: Wire the wizard and the preview**

- **State, hydration and `listSavesRef`**, as in Interfaces.
- **The body.** `income_info` is the ten, then `marital_status: incomeData.marital_status ?? null`. `household` is `{ members: membersToSend(householdRows, noOneElse) }`. Under `married_together` only, `spouse_income` is each field `?? null`. The deps gain the three new states. The comment at `:252-255` loses its blank `marital_status` sentence.
- **`saveCurrentStepData`**, when `household.members` is an array:

  1. send `members.map(withoutKey)`;
  2. take `++listSavesRef.current.members` before the PATCH;
  3. if the reply is stale, return;
  4. otherwise pair `savedIds(members, saved.household?.members as { id: number }[] | undefined)`, then call `setHouseholdRows((rows) => withSavedIds(rows, ids))`. The cast is needed because `HouseholdMember.id` is optional and the list may be `null`, and it is safe because the server returns every row with its id. A `null` reply fails the length check in `savedIds` (`:65`).
  5. return `{ ...body, household: { members: withSavedIds(members, ids).map(sentMember) } }`.

  A `null` list skips all of this.

- **Render.** Pass the six props.
- **The preview.** `:110` becomes `<dt>{MEANS_TEST_INCOME_LABEL}</dt>`, and `:114` becomes `<dt>{MEDIAN_FOR_HOUSEHOLD_OF} {result.family_size}</dt>`.

```text
feat(intake): ask marriage, the household and the spouse's income first

The Income step opens with Form 122A-1 line 1, then the people in the
filer's home and those they support, then the filer's ten amounts and,
for a filer living with their spouse, the spouse's ten. A half-finished
row saves with the step; an empty list is sent only when the filer says
no one. The wizard holds the rows, so a new row keeps its id and showing
the step again sends nothing. The journeys answer the new questions.
```

### Task 7: Wiring: the means test, Complete, the counts and `number_of_dependents`

The household list starts to decide things. The means test refuses with the first Income-step gap. When it doesn't refuse, it adds the spouse's income for `married_together`, and it sizes the median by line 13's count. Complete names every new gap. The session read returns the two counts. `number_of_dependents` is removed with its readers, and `DebtorInfo.household_size` stops being read. Line numbers are at `85dc976`. T1, T2 and T4 shift some of them, so each is also named by its symbol.

**Files:**

- Create:
  - `backend/apps/intake/migrations/0019_remove_incomeinfo_number_of_dependents.py`, generated;
  - `backend/apps/eligibility/tests/test_household_means_test.py`.
- Modify:
  - `backend/apps/eligibility/models.py`:
    - the import `:12`;
    - `MeansTest.calculate` `:98-128`, its docstring and the household-size block;
    - `:186-215`, the details.
  - `backend/apps/eligibility/services/expense_deduction_calculator.py`: the import `:6`, `_get_family_size` `:69-80`, and `_get_cmi` `:112-117`.
  - `backend/apps/eligibility/services/means_test_calculator.py:209-213`, `family_composition`.
  - `backend/apps/eligibility/cmi.py`: the module docstring `:1-9` (Step 6).
  - `backend/apps/intake/models.py`: `IncomeInfo.number_of_dependents` `:121` and `IncomeInfo.__str__` `:207-211`.
  - `backend/apps/intake/serializers.py`:
    - `IncomeInfoSerializer.Meta.fields` `:85-92`;
    - T1's `HouseholdSerializer`.
  - `backend/apps/intake/views.py`: the imports `:15` and `:17`, and Complete `:236-250`.
  - `backend/apps/forms/registry.py`: `_needs_county` goes after `_needs_ssn`, `:79-85`.
  - `backend/apps/forms/views.py`: the import `:25`, and `_recalculate_means_test` `:159-170`.
  - `backend/apps/forms/services/derivations.py`:
    - the import `:18`;
    - `_cmi` `:149-157`;
    - a new `_family_size` beside `_line13a_median_income` `:349`;
    - `DERIVATIONS["family_size"]` `:552`;
    - `RULE_FIXES["family_size"]` `:694`, which moves under `# Income` `:695`.
  - `backend/apps/intake/management/commands/seed_demo_data.py`: the six `"number_of_dependents"` keys, `:73`, `:194`, `:274`, `:348`, `:456` and `:526`.
- Test: see the steps. The breaks are in the table below.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes:
  - **T3, `apps.intake.household`:**
    - `count_family_size(members: list[dict]) -> int`
    - `count_exemptions(members: list[dict]) -> int`
    - `count_fee_waiver_household(members: list[dict], marital_status: str | None) -> int`
  - **T3, `apps.eligibility.cmi`:**
    - `class IncomeStepGap(IncomeNotSplit)`, with `step = "income_info"`;
    - `household_cmi(session) -> Decimal`, which raises `IncomeNotSplit()` when there is no income row;
    - `spouse_monthly_income(session) -> Decimal | None`;
    - `household_members(session) -> list[dict]`, which raises `IncomeStepGap(HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED)` when there is no row or the list is null;
    - `income_step_gaps(session) -> list[IncomeStepGap]`, which never raises.
  - **T3, `apps.intake.tests.builders`:** `answer_household(session, *, marital_status: str | None = "not_married", members: Sequence[dict] | None = (), county: str | None = "Cook", spouse_income: Mapping[str, Decimal | None] | None = None) -> Household`, which needs an income row.
  - **T1:**
    - `Household`, with `session.household`, `county`, `get_members(self) -> list[dict] | None` and `store_members(self, rows: list[dict] | None) -> list[dict] | None`, which doesn't save;
    - `HouseholdSerializer`, whose `to_representation` override must let the two new method fields through;
    - the API key `household`.
  - **T2:** `SpouseIncome`, read as `session.spouse_income`; the four marriage answers.
  - **T4:** the seed's persona `household` and `spouse_income` keys.
- Produces:
  - **`MeansTest.calculate(self) -> bool`**, signature unchanged:
    - It first raises `income_step_gaps(self.session)[0]` when the list isn't empty.
    - Then `cmi = household_cmi(self.session)` and `family_size = count_family_size(household_members(self.session))`.
    - It never reads `DebtorInfo.household_size`.
    - `calculation_details` drops `number_of_dependents`. `marital_status` holds the line 1 answer, `family_size` holds line 13's count, and `income_by_source` keeps the filer's ten. It gains `"spouse_cmi": float | None`, from `spouse_monthly_income`.
  - **`ExpenseDeductionCalculator`:**
    - `_get_family_size(self) -> int` is `count_exemptions(household_members(self.session))`. On any exception it returns `1` (DIG-152). It no longer reads `DebtorInfo`.
    - `_get_cmi(self) -> Decimal` still returns `Decimal("0")` with no income row. Otherwise it returns `household_cmi(self.session)` (P20).
  - **`MeansTestCalculator.get_detailed_breakdown()["family_composition"]`** is `{"marital_status": <answer>, "total_family_size": <int>}`.
  - **`apps.forms.registry._needs_county(session: IntakeSession) -> None`** raises `PreconditionMissing("Choose your county on the Your Information step.", step="debtor_info")` when the session has no `Household` or its `county` is empty. It doesn't call `_needs_debtor_info`. T10 consumes it.
  - **Complete's errors, in order:**
    1. the `_needs_ssn` block, unchanged;
    2. its own `try: _needs_county(session)`;
    3. `[{"step": g.step, "message": str(g)} for g in income_step_gaps(session)]`;
    4. expenses;
    5. debts.
  - **`forms.views._recalculate_means_test(session) -> None`** raises `income_step_gaps(session)[0]` when there is a gap, then runs the calculator.
  - **`derivations`:**
    - `_family_size(session: IntakeSession) -> str` is `str(count_family_size(household_members(session)))`, and `DERIVATIONS["family_size"] = _family_size`;
    - `_cmi` returns `_fmt(household_cmi(session))`;
    - `RULE_FIXES["family_size"] == {"step": "income_info"}`.
  - **`HouseholdSerializer`:**
    - `family_size` and `fee_waiver_household_size` are `SerializerMethodField`s, and each `get_…(self, obj: Household) -> int | None`.
    - Each is None while `income_step_gaps(obj.session)` isn't empty. Otherwise they are `count_family_size(members)` and `count_fee_waiver_household(members, obj.session.income_info.marital_status)`.
    - `Meta.fields` is `["county", "members", "family_size", "fee_waiver_household_size"]`.
  - **API, consumed by T11:** every session read, the PATCH/PUT reply, and `update_step`'s and Complete's `session` carry `household: {county, members, family_size, fee_waiver_household_size}`, or `null` when the session has no `Household`.
  - **`IncomeInfo`** has no `number_of_dependents`. Migration `0019` depends on `0018_spouseincome`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing tests**

All sentences are the spec's §9.1 words, typed out in each test file and never imported, so a wrong constant fails the test. The persona rows are as stored, for example `SPOUSE_44 = {"relationship": "spouse", "age": 44, "lives_with_you": True, "claimed_or_supported": True, "spouse_is_dependent": False}`.

`backend/apps/eligibility/tests/test_household_means_test.py` (new):

- Imports: `date` (`datetime`, for `_debtor`'s birth date), `Decimal`, `pytest`, `call_command`, `APIClient`; `District` (`apps.districts.models`); `IncomeStepGap` (`apps.eligibility.cmi`); `MeansTest` (`apps.eligibility.models`); `ExpenseDeductionCalculator` and `MeansTestCalculator` (`apps.eligibility.services.expense_deduction_calculator` and `.means_test_calculator`); `GeneratedForm` (`apps.forms.models`); `DebtorInfo`, `ExpenseInfo`, `Household`, `IncomeInfo`, `IntakeSession` and `SpouseIncome` (`apps.intake.models`); `answer_household` (`apps.intake.tests.builders`); `User` (`apps.users.models`).
- `MARRIAGE` and `HOUSEHOLD_LIST` are §9.1's marriage and list sentences, typed out.
- Its fixture `filer(db)` loads `ilnd_2025_data` and takes `District.objects.get(code="ilnd")`, as `test_form_views.py:93-97` does. It returns `(client, session)`: an `APIClient` authenticated as a new `User`, and a new `IntakeSession` of that user in that district. On 2026-10-16 that table's medians are 73,180, 93,934, 113,625 and 137,902.
- `_url(session)` is `f"/api/intake/sessions/{session.pk}/"`.
- `_answer(session, marital_status, members, wages, spouse_wages=None)`:
  - creates `IncomeInfo` with all ten typed: `Decimal("0.00")`, and the wages;
  - then calls `answer_household(session, marital_status=…, members=…, spouse_income=<the ten typed, wages=spouse_wages> or None)`.
- `_debtor(session, **fields)` creates a `DebtorInfo` with the fields `test_means_test_calculator.py:871-884` gives, but phone `312-555-0100`, then `fields`.
- Each test opens with `client, session = filer`. A test that names a persona tuple then calls `_answer(session, *<tuple>)`, which runs the builder. The tests that name no persona (the pre-3e1 ones, the null counts and the PATCH) build their rows by hand and never call it.
- The rows, defined before the tuples: `SPOUSE_45` and `SPOUSE_44` (Y, Y, `spouse_is_dependent` False); `DAUGHTER_10`, `SON_7`, `SON_8` and `DAUGHTER_5` (Y, Y); `ROOMMATE_29` (Y, N); `SON_19_ELSEWHERE_SUPPORTED` (`lives_with_you` False, `claimed_or_supported` True).

```python
PRIYA = ("married_together", [SPOUSE_45, DAUGHTER_10, SON_7], "12000.00", "1800.00")
DESHAWN = ("married_together", [SPOUSE_44], "3500.00", "1000.00")
SARAH = ("not_married", [ROOMMATE_29], "2000.00", None)  # roommate: lives with her, not claimed
MARIA = ("not_married", [SON_8, DAUGHTER_5], "3200.00", None)

def test_a_filer_living_with_their_spouse_is_compared_to_the_median_on_both_incomes(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *PRIYA)
    result = MeansTestCalculator(session).calculate()
    assert result["cmi"] == Decimal("13800.00")
    assert result["details"]["annualized_cmi"] == 165600.0
    assert result["median_income_threshold"] == Decimal("137902.00")
    assert (result["passes_means_test"], result["above_median_calculated"]) == (False, True)

def test_sarahs_roommate_counts_in_her_household_and_her_median_follows(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *SARAH)
    result = MeansTestCalculator(session).calculate()
    assert (result["family_size"], result["median_income_threshold"]) == (2, Decimal("93934.00"))

def test_deshawn_still_qualifies_for_the_fee_flag_on_both_incomes(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *DESHAWN)
    result = MeansTestCalculator(session).calculate()
    # 54,000.00 a year is under 60% of 93,934.00 (56,360.40)
    assert (result["cmi"], result["details"]["annualized_cmi"]) == (Decimal("4500.00"), 54000.0)
    assert result["qualifies_for_fee_waiver"] is True

@pytest.mark.parametrize("answered,refusal", [(False, MARRIAGE), (True, HOUSEHOLD_LIST)])
def test_no_intake_completed_before_3e1_is_given_a_household_of_one(filer, answered, refusal):
    client, session = filer
    # income saved before 3e1, by hand: no Household; `answered` sets not_married, else null
    IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, marital_status="not_married" if answered else None,
                              monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("3000.00"))
    with pytest.raises(IncomeStepGap) as refused:
        MeansTestCalculator(session).calculate()
    assert str(refused.value) == refusal
    assert not MeansTest.objects.filter(session=session).exists()

def test_calculate_means_test_returns_the_first_income_step_gap(filer):
    client, session = filer
    IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("3000.00"))  # pre-3e1, by hand
    response = client.post(f"{_url(session)}calculate_means_test/")
    assert (response.status_code, response.data["error"]) == (400, MARRIAGE)
```

`_completed_before_3e1(session)` gives the filer's session, by hand and never with the builder:

- `status="completed"`;
- `_debtor(session, ssn="900-12-3456")`, since `date_of_birth` can't be null;
- `IncomeInfo(monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("3000.00"))`, whose marriage answer is null;
- `ExpenseInfo()`;
- no `Household`.

```python
REFUSED = {"error": MARRIAGE, "step": "income_info"}

def _422(response):
    assert response.status_code == 422, response.data
    return response.data

def test_an_intake_completed_before_3e1_refuses_to_generate_and_names_the_income_step(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _completed_before_3e1(session)
    form = GeneratedForm.objects.create(session=session, form_type="form_106dec", status="generated", generated_by=session.user)
    # the whole call: no form_type in the body
    assert _422(client.post("/api/forms/generate_all/", {"session_id": session.pk}, format="json")) == REFUSED
    assert _422(client.get("/api/forms/packet/", {"session": session.pk})) == REFUSED
    # one form: the body names it
    one = {"form_type": "form_106dec", **REFUSED}
    assert _422(client.post("/api/forms/generate/", {"session_id": session.pk, "form_type": "form_106dec"}, format="json")) == one
    assert _422(client.post(f"/api/forms/{form.pk}/regenerate/")) == one
    assert _422(client.get(f"/api/forms/{form.pk}/download/")) == one

def test_a_filed_form_with_no_stored_values_refuses_like_an_unfiled_one(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _completed_before_3e1(session)
    # Review Focus 4: filed before 3e1 with field_values "" draws from the current intake, so step survives
    form = GeneratedForm.objects.create(session=session, form_type="form_106dec", status="filed", field_values="", generated_by=session.user)
    assert _422(client.get(f"/api/forms/{form.pk}/download/")) == {"form_type": "form_106dec", **REFUSED}
```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("household,counts", [(MARIA, (3, 3)), (DESHAWN, (2, 2)), (SARAH, (2, 1))], ids=["maria", "deshawn", "sarah"])
def test_the_session_read_returns_the_households_counts(filer, household, counts):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *household)
    got = client.get(_url(session)).data["household"]
    assert (got["family_size"], got["fee_waiver_household_size"]) == counts

def test_the_counts_are_null_while_a_gap_remains_and_a_bare_session_reads_without_error(filer):
    client, session = filer
    url = _url(session)
    assert client.get(url).data["household"] is None
    IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, marital_status="not_married", monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("2000.00"))
    Household.objects.create(session=session, county="Cook")   # step 1 saved, list never answered
    got = client.get(url).data["household"]
    assert (got["family_size"], got["fee_waiver_household_size"]) == (None, None)

def test_a_patch_that_answers_the_income_step_returns_its_counts_in_the_reply(filer):
    client, session = filer
    url = _url(session)
    # Review Focus 1. Stored by hand before this PATCH, as autosave leaves them: the gaps' old answers.
    Household.objects.create(session=session, county="Cook")
    IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("3500.00"))
    SpouseIncome.objects.create(session=session)
    reply = client.patch(url, {"income_info": {"marital_status": "married_together"},
                               "household": {"members": [SPOUSE_44]},
                               "spouse_income": {"monthly_gross_wages": "1000.00"}}, format="json")
    assert reply.status_code == 200
    assert (reply.data["household"]["family_size"], reply.data["household"]["fee_waiver_household_size"]) == (2, 2)
```

The expense-allowance cases: every case also sets `DebtorInfo.household_size=3` and `ExpenseInfo(food_and_groceries=Decimal("1200"))`. Food is the lesser of 1,200 and the IRS food figure for the size: 713, 904, 1,088 and 1,289 for 1 to 4 people. A size read from line 13 or from the debtor box therefore gives a different food figure.

The spouse's-income case pins §5.3's switch of `_get_cmi` to `household_cmi`. Disposable income is `cmi - total_allowable - priority` (`expense_deduction_calculator.py:54-55`), and nothing else there reads the CMI:

- each allowance is the lesser of the actual expense and its standard, which reads `family_size`, the debtor's age or the district, never the CMI (`:30-39`, `:43-46`);
- `priority` comes from the confirmed priority debts (`:52`, `:105-110`).

So, with the same household and expenses, spouse wages of 1800.00 make disposable income exactly 1800.00 higher than spouse wages of 0.00 (Priya: 13,800.00 against 12,000.00 of CMI). A filer-only `_get_cmi` gives a difference of 0.00.

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("household,size,food", [
    (PRIYA, 4, "1200"),
    (SARAH, 1, "713"),                                            # line 13 is 2
    (("not_married", [SON_19_ELSEWHERE_SUPPORTED], "2000.00", None), 2, "904"),   # line 13 is 1
], ids=["priya", "a_roommate_lives_there_but_isnt_claimed", "a_child_supported_elsewhere"])
def test_the_expense_allowances_use_line_5s_count(filer, household, size, food):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *household)
    _debtor(session, household_size=3)  # the old box, which no count reads
    ExpenseInfo.objects.create(session=session, food_and_groceries=Decimal("1200"))
    result = ExpenseDeductionCalculator(session).calculate()
    assert (result.family_size, result.national_food_allowance) == (size, Decimal(food))

def test_the_expense_calculator_counts_the_spouses_income(filer):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *PRIYA)  # spouse wages 1800.00, the other nine typed $0
    ExpenseInfo.objects.create(session=session, food_and_groceries=Decimal("1200"))
    with_spouse = ExpenseDeductionCalculator(session).calculate()
    # The same household, the spouse's ten all typed $0 (all None would be the spouse-income gap).
    answer_household(session, marital_status=PRIYA[0], members=PRIYA[1],
                     spouse_income=dict.fromkeys(IncomeInfo.SOURCE_FIELDS, Decimal("0.00")))
    without = ExpenseDeductionCalculator(session).calculate()
    assert (with_spouse.family_size, with_spouse.allowable_expenses) == (without.family_size, without.allowable_expenses)
    assert with_spouse.disposable_income - without.disposable_income == Decimal("1800.00")

@pytest.mark.parametrize("household,family_size,spouse_cmi", [(PRIYA, 4, 1800.0), (SARAH, 2, None)], ids=["priya", "sarah"])
def test_the_means_test_stores_the_marriage_answer_line_13_and_column_b(filer, household, family_size, spouse_cmi):
    client, session = filer
    _answer(session, *household)
    details = MeansTestCalculator(session).calculate()["details"]
    assert (details["marital_status"], details["family_size"], details["spouse_cmi"]) == (household[0], family_size, spouse_cmi)
    assert "number_of_dependents" not in details
```

`backend/apps/intake/tests/test_completion.py`:

- Add the seven sentences, import `SpouseIncome` beside the other models (`:20-27`), and type out the rows the mutators use: `SPOUSE_44` and a son of 8 (Y, Y).
- The mutators work on `completed`, whose builder already stored `not_married`, `[]` and Cook:
  - `_marry(session, answer)` sets the answer and saves;
  - `_household_list(session, rows)` runs `store_members([dict(r) for r in rows])` and saves;
  - `_spouse_typed(session)` creates a `SpouseIncome` with `monthly_gross_wages=Decimal("0.00")`.
- Each `_<gap>(session)` below is a short mutator built from those three, as the 3a1 gaps are (`:181-199`); the comment beside it says what it sets.
- This file's `filer` returns `(client, user)` (`:41-46`), so the test opens as the 3a1 test does (`:228-235`).

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("gap,step,message", [
    (_no_county, "debtor_info", COUNTY),                                   # household.county = None
    (_marriage_unanswered, "income_info", MARRIAGE),
    (_spouse_filing_with_you, "income_info", SPOUSE_FILING),               # married_spouse_filing
    (_household_list_unanswered, "income_info", HOUSEHOLD_LIST),           # members None
    (_a_row_half_answered, "income_info", HOUSEHOLD_ROW),                  # son 8, lives_with_you None
    (_a_spouse_row_but_not_married, "income_info", MISMATCH),              # [SPOUSE_44] under not_married
    (_living_together_with_twenty_others_and_no_spouse, "income_info", MISMATCH),  # Review Focus 3: 20 answered non-spouse rows (e.g. 20 copies of son 8, Y, Y), spouse typed $0
    (_spouse_income_not_entered, "income_info", SPOUSE_INCOME),            # married_together, [SPOUSE_44], SpouseIncome with no amounts
])
def test_complete_names_every_new_gap_by_its_step(filer, completed, gap, step, message):
    client, _ = filer
    completed.status = "in_progress"
    completed.save()
    gap(completed)
    response = client.post(_session_url(completed, "complete/"))
    assert response.data["errors"] == [{"step": step, "message": message}]
```

Every other test in these files passes.

- [ ] **Step 6: The means test reads the household**

As Produces says, plus:

- `MeansTest.calculate` deletes the household-size block (`:119-128`, both branches) and the now unreachable `try/except AttributeError` (`:111-114`). Its docstring's Raises gains `IncomeStepGap`.
- `_get_family_size` deletes its `DebtorInfo` branch (`:70-72`). It sits outside the `try`, and `household_size` defaults to 1, so it would win for every filer.
- `_get_cmi` keeps its no-income-row 0, which `test_expense_deduction_calculator.py:15-42` and `:83-96` rely on (P20).
- `current_monthly_income` leaves both modules' imports.
- `cmi.py`'s module docstring (`:1-9`) stops calling `current_monthly_income` "the one figure the means test, the above-median calculator and the court forms share". It says instead that the means test, the above-median check, the expense calculator and the forms' `cmi` rule read the household's CMI, `household_cmi`: the filer's plus the spouse's for `married_together`, the filer's alone otherwise (3e1 spec §5.3). `current_monthly_income` works out one person's ten amounts, without Social Security, rounded to the cent, so each column and their sum print and compare the same everywhere.

- [ ] **Step 7: Complete, `_needs_county`, the forms' recalculation, and the lock test's pause**

As Produces says, plus:

- `intake/views.py` imports `income_step_gaps` by name, so the patch below can resolve it, and drops `current_monthly_income`.
- `_recalculate_means_test`'s docstring says it raises the first income-step gap.
- In `test_session_lock.py:242-256`, `_complete_pauses_in_its_checks` patches `intake_views.income_step_gaps` with a `check_gaps(session)`. It sets `checking`, waits for `go_on`, and then returns the real gaps.

- [ ] **Step 8: 13B and the CMI rule**

As Produces says. `RULE_FIXES["family_size"]` moves to the top of `# Income`. At `derivations.py:18`, `IncomeNotSplit` and `current_monthly_income` have no other use, so drop them; ruff fails on unused names.

- [ ] **Step 9: The counts**

The counts go on T1's `HouseholdSerializer`, as Produces says, and its `to_representation` emits them. Add no `refresh_from_db()`: Tasks 1 and 2 point the session's cached `household`, `income_info` and `spouse_income` at the rows the save wrote, so Step 3's PATCH test reads this save's answers (P16).

```text
feat(eligibility): the household decides the means test and Complete

The means test adds the spouse's income when they live together and
sizes the median by everyone living with the filer (122A-1 line 13);
the expense allowances use 122A-2 line 5's count. It first refuses
with the first Income-step gap, so an intake completed before 3e1 is
never sized at one: every forms call that works it out is a 422 naming
the Income step.

Complete names a missing county and each new Income-step gap. Session
reads and saves return the line 13 and 103B line 1 counts, null while
a gap remains. IncomeInfo.number_of_dependents is removed (0019), and
DebtorInfo.household_size is no longer read.
```

```python
                  "claimed_or_supported": True, "spouse_is_dependent": False}
# Each spouse rule and the filer rule it mirrors: the same sources, the spouse's row.
SPOUSE_TWINS = {
    "line1_wages_spouse": "line1_wages", "line3_alimony_spouse": "line3_alimony",
    "line6b_child_support_alimony_spouse": "line6b_child_support_alimony",
    "line3_real_property_income_spouse": "line3_real_property_income",
    "line6a_unemployment_spouse": "line6a_unemployment",
    "line5a_pension_retirement_spouse": "line5a_pension_retirement",
    "line7_other_income_spouse": "line7_other_income", "spouse_cmi": "filer_cmi",
    **{f"schedule_i_{n}_spouse": f"schedule_i_{n}_debtor1" for n in (
        "net_wages", "overtime", "gross_income", "rental", "child_support", "unemployment",
        "social_security", "pension", "other_income", "other_income_total", "income_total")},
}

def _spouse_at_home(session, marital_status="married_together", **spouse):
    # The filer's ten are typed $0, so a spouse rule reading the filer's row prints 0.00.
    IncomeInfo.objects.create(session=session, **dict.fromkeys(TEN_SOURCES, Decimal("0")))
    answer_household(session, marital_status=marital_status, members=[SPOUSE_AT_HOME],
                     spouse_income=spouse)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("rule,twin", list(SPOUSE_TWINS.items()))
def test_a_spouse_at_home_prints_their_own_figure_on_each_line_the_filer_does(session, rule, twin):
    _spouse_at_home(session, **TEN_SOURCES)
    expected = sum((TEN_SOURCES[f] for f in SWITCHED_RULES[twin]), Decimal("0"))
    assert DERIVATIONS[rule](session) == f"{expected:.2f}"

@pytest.mark.parametrize("rule", [r for r in SPOUSE_TWINS if r != "spouse_cmi"])
def test_a_spouse_line_prints_blank_when_the_spouse_gave_none_of_its_sources(session, rule):
    _spouse_at_home(session, **{f: a for f, a in TEN_SOURCES.items()
                                if f not in SWITCHED_RULES[SPOUSE_TWINS[rule]]})
    assert DERIVATIONS[rule](session) == ""

@pytest.mark.parametrize("rule", list(SPOUSE_TWINS))
def test_a_spouse_source_typed_as_zero_prints_zero(session, rule):
    _spouse_at_home(session, **dict.fromkeys(TEN_SOURCES, Decimal("0")))
    assert DERIVATIONS[rule](session) == "0.00"

@pytest.mark.parametrize("answer", ["married_separately", "not_married"])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("rule", list(SPOUSE_TWINS))
def test_a_spouse_not_at_home_prints_nothing_in_their_column(session, rule, answer):
    _spouse_at_home(session, marital_status=answer, **TEN_SOURCES)  # saved, not counted (§5.1)
    assert DERIVATIONS[rule](session) == ""
```

```python
LINE_10_SOURCE = "terrorism If necessary list other sources on a separate page and put the total below 1"

def answer_income_step(session, marital_status, spouse_income=None, **amounts):

])

])
```

### Task 9: The engine: the household list binding, and a box's answer on a sheet

Schedule J line 2 becomes a repeat group in Task 10. This task makes the engine able to carry it, and proves it on a synthetic Schedule J group shaped exactly as Task 10 declares it (spec §10.3, changes 1 and 2). No production schema binds `household.` after this task.

**Files:**

- Modify: `backend/apps/forms/field_index.py:33-46` (`IndexWidget`), `:69-79` (the loader), and a new `option_text()` after `load_index` (`:53-81`).
- Modify: `backend/apps/forms/services/fill_resolver.py:14-17` (imports), `:33-60` (`resolve_binding`), `:111-129` (`fix_for`), `:257-293` (`_sheet`) and its one call, `:375-387`.
- Modify: `backend/apps/forms/schema.py:88` (the `SheetColumn.binding` comment), `:199`, `:406-451` (with the comments at `:407-410` and `:429-433`), `:580` and `:624-631`.
- Modify: `backend/apps/forms/tests/conftest.py`: two fixtures after `schedule_d_with_a_copy_and_a_sheet` (`:231-243`).
- Test: `backend/apps/forms/tests/test_field_index.py`, `test_fill_resolver.py` (after `:71`), `test_rule_fixes.py:186-194`, `test_checks.py` (`:926-931`, `:942-991`, the ids at `:1049-1072`, and new tests after `:769-770` and `:1074-1078`), `test_attached_sheets.py` (two tests at the end), and `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_bulk_answers.py` (one method in `TestBulkAnswerView`).

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes:
  - From T1: `apps.intake.household.RELATIONSHIP_WORDS: dict[str, str]`, `ANSWER_KEYS = ("relationship", "age", "lives_with_you", "claimed_or_supported")` and `SPOUSE_KEY = "spouse_is_dependent"`. Also `apps.intake.models.Household` (`session` one-to-one, `related_name="household"`) and `Household.store_members(self, rows: list[dict] | None) -> list[dict] | None`, which normalises and numbers the rows but doesn't save.
  - From T3: `apps.intake.household.dependents(members: list[dict]) -> list[dict]` and `apps.eligibility.cmi.household_members(session) -> list[dict]`. `household_members` reads `session.household`, the reverse one-to-one, which Django caches on the session instance, a missing row included. It raises `IncomeStepGap(HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED)` when there is no row or the list is null. Also `IncomeStepGap` and `HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED`.
  - From T4: `generated_form_factory` answers the household with `members=[]`. This task's fixture uses `get_or_create`, so it doesn't depend on that.
- Produces:
  - `apps.forms.field_index.IndexWidget.option_text: str`, loaded as `row["option_text"] or ""`.
  - `apps.forms.field_index.option_text(template_filename: str, full_name: str, state: str) -> str`. It returns the `option_text` of the widget of `full_name` whose `export_state(on_state) == state`, and raises `KeyError` when none matches.
  - `apps.forms.schema._BINDING_NAMESPACES = ("answer:", "sofa.", "household.")`.
  - `apps.forms.schema._LIST_BINDING = re.compile(r"(?:sofa\.\w+|household\.dependents)\[\]\.\w+")`.
  - A household branch in `resolve_binding`. `household.dependents[].<attr>`, with `attr` one of `relationship`, `age` or `lives_with_you`, returns a `list[str]` over `dependents(household_members(session))`. Any other `household.` binding raises `ValueError(f"unrecognized binding: {binding!r}")` before anything is read.
  - `fix_for(field)` returns `{"step": "income_info"}` for a binding starting `household.`.
  - `_sheet(group, fields, printed, columns, capacity, actual, session, ingested_cache, template_filename: str) -> dict`. A box slot's cell is `option_text(template_filename, slots[0].pdf_field, emitted)`, or `""` when nothing is emitted.
  - Test fixtures in `apps/forms/tests/conftest.py`: `schedule_j_with_dependents` (yields the schemas directory) and `household_list`, which returns `store(session, *rows) -> IntakeSession`.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:**

- [ ] **Step 3: Add `IndexWidget.option_text` and `option_text()` in `apps/forms/field_index.py`**

`option_text: str` is the dataclass's last field, documented as the words the form prints beside a box (`""` for a text box). `option_text(template_filename: str, full_name: str, state: str) -> str` matches the state through `export_state`, since the index stores the bare `yes`. It skips widgets whose `on_state` is None, because `export_state(None)` raises `TypeError`, so a name with no matching box raises `KeyError`, as the test asks.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the failing tests for the household binding**

In `conftest.py`, add the setter, importing `Household` from `apps.intake.models` and `ANSWER_KEYS` and `SPOUSE_KEY` from `apps.intake.household`. It writes the list through T1's model API, so it works whether or not the session already has a `Household`. It also hands back a fresh session, so no read is cached:

```python
@pytest.fixture
def household_list():
    """
    store(session, *rows): the session's household list, each row (relationship, age,
    lives_with_you, claimed_or_supported[, spouse_is_dependent]); returns the session read
    afresh from the database.
    """

    def store(session, *rows):
        household, _ = Household.objects.get_or_create(session=session)
        household.store_members([dict(zip((*ANSWER_KEYS, SPOUSE_KEY), row)) for row in rows])
        household.save()
        return IntakeSession.objects.get(pk=session.pk)

    return store
```

In `test_fill_resolver.py`, after `:71`, importing `HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED` and `IncomeStepGap` from `apps.eligibility.cmi`. These tests use the file's own bare `session`, which has no `IncomeInfo` and no `Household`:

```python
PRIYA = [("spouse", 45, True, True, False), ("daughter", 10, True, True), ("son", 7, True, True)]


def test_line_2s_rows_come_from_the_household_in_the_filers_order(session, household_list):
    session = household_list(session, *PRIYA)
    # Her husband is on their joint return, but she says he isn't her dependent (3e1.Q1).

    assert resolve_binding("household.dependents[].age", session) == ["10", "7"]
    assert resolve_binding("household.dependents[].lives_with_you", session) == ["yes", "yes"]

    for attr in ("relationship", "age", "lives_with_you"):
        assert resolve_binding(f"household.dependents[].{attr}", session) == [""]


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "binding", ["household.county", "household.dependents[].name", "household.members[].age"]
)
def test_an_unknown_household_binding_is_refused(session, binding):
    # The bare session has no list: an unknown binding is refused before anything is read.
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unrecognized binding"):
        resolve_binding(binding, session)


def test_a_household_never_answered_refuses_in_the_income_steps_words(session):

        resolve_binding("household.dependents[].age", session)
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Add the household branch to `resolve_binding` and the household case to `fix_for`**

In `fill_resolver.py`, import `household_members` from `apps.eligibility.cmi`, and `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS` and `dependents` from `apps.intake.household`.

The branch splits the path at `"[]."` and checks that the collection is `dependents` and the attribute is one of the three. It refuses any other binding before it reads the list, so a refusal never reaches `household_members`.

Its rows are `dependents(household_members(session))`, in stored order. `household_members`'s `IncomeStepGap` passes through unchanged: an unanswered list is a gap (§9.1), never "no dependents". Each cell is converted like this:

| `attr`           | value → cell                                      |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| `relationship`   | `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS[v]`; `""` for None            |
| `age`            | `str(v)`, so 0 prints `"0"`; `""` for None        |
| `lives_with_you` | `"yes"` for True, `"no"` for False; `""` for None |

Never copy the SOFA path's `str(getattr(...))`, which prints "None" (facts FE F2).

The docstring gains the line `"household.dependents[].<attr>" → list of str over Schedule J line 2's dependents`. In `fix_for`, a binding starting `household.` returns `{"step": "income_info"}`, after the `sofa.` case.

The fixture `schedule_j_with_dependents(tmp_path, settings)` works like this:

- It copies the real schemas into `tmp_path / "schemas"` unless an earlier fixture of the test has, as `_with_group` does.
- It drops every field of `schedule_j.json` whose `pdf_field` is one of the 15 boxes above. T10 binds them; dropping them keeps this fixture valid after T10.
- For row n (1 to 5), it appends three slots, each with:
  - `"source": "asked"`, `"page": 1`, `"repeat": "j_line_2"`, `"repeat_capacity": 5`, `"row": n` and `"legal_review": False`;
  - every other key of `_with_group`'s slot dict, with `"label"` the box's name.
- Each slot's type and binding:
  - the relationship box: `"type": "text"`, `household.dependents[].relationship`;
  - the age box: `"type": "text"`, `household.dependents[].age`;
  - the "lives with you?" box: `"type": "checkbox"`, `household.dependents[].lives_with_you`, with `"choices": {"yes": "/yes", "no": "/no"}`.
- It sets `schema.setdefault("groups", {})["j_line_2"] = {"number": "2.{n}", "sheet": _J_SHEET}`.
- It points `FORM_SCHEMAS_DIRECTORY` there and clears `load_schema`'s cache before and after.

In `test_checks.py`, after `test_a_group_continued_on_an_attached_sheet_passes` (`:769-770`):

```text
feat(forms): the household list binding, and a box's answer on a sheet

Schedule J line 2 becomes the first production repeat group next, with
dependents past five on an attached sheet (3e1 spec §10.3). This adds
the two engine changes it needs, proven on a synthetic copy of the group.

household.dependents[].<attr> gives line 2's rows in the filer's order:
the relationship in the forms' words, the age, and yes or no. A blank
answer prints blank, and a list never answered refuses in the Income
step's words. The list is read once per form.

A sheet column may now print a box's answer, as the words the form
prints beside the box ("Yes", "No"). Startup refuses a column bound by
a box that doesn't give every answer its own state: an unmapped box, or
a lone box whose No would print blank.
```

---

### Task 10: Schedule J line 2, line 1 for every session, Form 101's County

Schedule J line 2 prints the household's dependents: `check2`, then rows a–e of the first production repeat group, `j_line_2`, whose rows past five continue on an attached sheet (3e1 spec §10.3). Line 1 answers No for every session (§7). Form 101's County prints the stored county, and Form 101 refuses without one (§9.2, §10.4). Line numbers are at 85dc976; Tasks 7 and 8 edit `derivations.py` and `registry.py` above these spots, so find them by name.

**Files:**

- Modify `data/forms/schemas/schedule_j.json`: a top-level `"groups"` after `template_version` (`:4`); 16 fields after `check1` (`:40-59`).
- Modify `data/forms/schemas/form_101.json:244-260` (`Debtor1.County`).
- Modify `backend/apps/forms/services/derivations.py`: the imports (`:18-19`); `_schedule_j_joint_case` (`:526-535`); a new `_schedule_j_has_dependents` after it; a new `_county`; `DERIVATIONS` (`:560`, `:665`); `RULE_FIXES` `# Your Information` (`:681-694`).
- Modify `backend/apps/forms/registry.py`: a new `_needs_debtor_info_and_county` after Task 7's `_needs_county`; `FORM_REGISTRY["form_101"]` (`:98`).
- Modify `data/forms/field_index/audit/step2_verification.json`: 17 rows, and the `check1` row's evidence.
- Test: `backend/apps/forms/tests/test_schedule_j_schema.py` (fixture `:58-68`, `:170-200` replaced), `test_schedule_i_schema.py:175-186`, `test_form_101_schema.py`, `test_form_views.py` (`TestGenerateEndpoint`, `:160`), `test_registry.py` (`:208-223`), `test_rule_fixes.py:160-175`, `test_oracle_tier2.py:37`, `test_oracle_tier3.py` (`:33-42`, `:45-257`), `test_api_readback.py:137-146`.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes:
  - Task 9: the `household.dependents[].<attr>` binding (`relationship` → `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS[v]`, `age` → `str(age)`, `lives_with_you` → `"yes"`/`"no"`, null → `""`); `fix_for` mapping `household.` to `{"step": "income_info"}`; a sheet cell for a box slot as the index's `option_text` (`"Yes"`/`"No"`); P18's sheet checks, which let a box with a state for each answer share a column.
  - Task 3: `dependents(members: list[dict]) -> list[dict]` (`apps.intake.household`); `household_members(session) -> list[dict]`, raising `IncomeStepGap(HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED)` with no row or a null list, and `IncomeStepGap`, `HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED` (`apps.eligibility.cmi`).
  - Task 7: `registry._needs_county(session: IntakeSession) -> None`, raising `PreconditionMissing("Choose your county on the Your Information step.", step="debtor_info")`.
  - Task 1: `Household` (`county`, `store_members(self, rows: list[dict] | None) -> list[dict] | None`); the builder from Task 3, `answer_household`, which Task 4 calls in `generated_form_factory` and `test_form_views.py`'s `session` (county `"Cook"`).
- Produces:
  - Group `j_line_2` in `schedule_j.json`.
  - `_schedule_j_has_dependents(session: IntakeSession) -> str`: `"yes"` when `dependents(household_members(session))` is non-empty, else `"no"`; it lets `IncomeStepGap` through for an unanswered list rather than swear to a No the filer never gave. That is narrower than §10.3's "No otherwise": the plan's call, listed for the owner (P19). Rule `schedule_j_has_dependents`, no `RULE_FIXES` entry (a box rule).
  - `_schedule_j_joint_case(session) -> str`: `"no"` always. Its docstring: line 1 says No for every session, since no step offers joint filing and Complete refuses a case the spouse files too (3e1 spec §7, DIG-177); `filing_type` has no reader left.
  - `_county(session: IntakeSession) -> str`: `getattr(session, "household", None)`'s `county`, or `""`. Rule `county`; `RULE_FIXES["county"] = {"step": "debtor_info"}`.
  - `registry._needs_debtor_info_and_county(session: IntakeSession) -> None`: `_needs_debtor_info(session)`, then `_needs_county(session)`; `FORM_REGISTRY["form_101"] = PacketForm(_always, _needs_debtor_info_and_county)`.

**Breaks and fixes at this boundary:**

- Line 2 reads the household through `household_members`, so from here a Schedule J resolve on a session with no `Household`, or a null list, raises `IncomeStepGap`. That breaks:
  - every test in `test_schedule_j_schema.py`, through its `session` fixture (`:58-68`). Several create only an `ExpenseInfo`, so `answer_household` (which needs an `IncomeInfo`) can't fix it: the fixture gives the session a `Household` answered "no one" through `_household` (Step 1);
  - `test_schedule_i_schema.py:175` (`test_income_lines_print_blank_until_the_filer_gives_income_by_source`), which resolves Schedule J before any `IncomeInfo` exists: the same three lines at its start.
  - `test_schedule_i_schema.py:163` resolves Schedule J too, but Task 7's Step 1 already gave it a household.
  - Every other Schedule J resolve or Generate in the suite is on a seeded persona (tier 3, `test_signing_lines.py`, `test_printed_page.py`, `test_coverage.py`, `test_api_readback.py`) or on a Task 4 fixture (`forms/tests/conftest.py` `generated_form_factory`, `test_form_views.py` `session`, `test_completion.py` `completed`, `test_time_packet.py`).
- `test_schedule_j_schema.py:170-200` pins a blank line 1 for a joint session and one with no Your Information step: replaced by `test_is_this_a_joint_case_answers_no_for_every_session`.
- Form 101 with no county: every view path works the means test out before `requires()`, and every fixture that passes the means test is Task 4's, with county `"Cook"`; `test_registry.py:165` reaches Form 101 with no debtor, and `_needs_debtor_info` still answers first. Two tests do break, since they call `build_field_values("form_101", session)` directly, with a debtor and no `Household`: `test_field_values_name_index_widgets` (`test_registry.py:208-213`) and `test_petition_prints_only_the_last_four_ssn_digits` (`:216-223`, two cases). Each gets `Household.objects.create(session=session, county="Cook")` after `_debtor(session)` (Step 1).
- `ASSERTED_CASES` 406 → 422: `check2` and the 15 slots (16); `Debtor1.County` loses its audit case (`asked:answer:form_101.county`) and gains the step2 row `derived:county` (net 0).
- `test_api_readback.py:141`'s "No production schema has a repeat group" is false from here: rewritten (Step 1).
- The `check1` step2 row's evidence says line 1 "stays blank" for a joint filer: rewritten (Step 5). Its key doesn't change, so no test would notice.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`test_schedule_j_schema.py`: import `Household`, `build_parts`, `PDFFormFiller`, `read_sheets`, and `IncomeStepGap`, `HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED`. The `session` fixture ends with `_household(session, [])` and the comment `# line 2 refuses a list never answered (3e1 spec §9.2): "no one" here`.

```python
def _household(session, members):
    # Change the instance the session holds: the rules read session.household, which a
    # one-to-one keeps cached, so a fresh copy from the database would leave it stale.
    household = getattr(session, "household", None) or Household(session=session)
    household.store_members(members)
    household.save()

def _member(relationship, age, lives_with_you=True, claimed_or_supported=True, **spouse):
    return {"relationship": relationship, "age": age, "lives_with_you": lives_with_you,
            "claimed_or_supported": claimed_or_supported, **spouse}

SIX = [_member("son", 15), _member("daughter", 12), _member("stepchild", 10), _member("grandchild", 4),
       _member("parent", 70), _member("other_relative", 30, lives_with_you=False)]

def test_line_2_holds_five_dependents_on_the_form(session):
    _household(session, SIX[:5])
    (form,) = build_parts("schedule_j", session)["parts"]  # no sheet
    rows = [tuple(form["values"][f"{box}{x}"] for box in ("Dependant Relation 2", "Dependant age 2", "check2"))
            for x in "abcde"]
    assert rows == [("Son", "15", "/yes"), ("Daughter", "12", "/yes"), ("Stepchild", "10", "/yes"),
                    ("Grandchild", "4", "/yes"), ("Parent", "70", "/yes")]

def test_a_sixth_dependent_continues_on_the_attached_sheet(generated_form_factory):
    # The template has no extra page for line 2, so row 6 goes on a sheet (U:145), "2.6".
    session = generated_form_factory().session
    _household(session, SIX)
    document = build_parts("schedule_j", session)
    assert document["parts"][1:] == [{
        "kind": "sheet", "group": "j_line_2",
        "title": "Official Form 106J, Schedule J: Your Expenses, line 2",
        "columns": [{"label": "Relationship", "width": 220.0}, {"label": "Age", "width": 80.0},
                    {"label": "Lives with you", "width": 168.0}],
        "name": "Maria Torres",
        "rows": [{"line": "2.6", "cells": ["Other relative", "30", "No"]}],
    }]
    PDFFormFiller().check("schedule_j", document)  # every row prints whole
    (sheet,) = read_sheets(PDFFormFiller().fill("schedule_j", document),
                           load_schema("schedule_j").groups["j_line_2"].sheet)
    assert sheet.rows == (("2.6", "Other relative", "30", "No"),)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("members,ticks,row_a", [
    ([], "/no", None),  # "no one", James's answer
    ([_member("roommate", 29, claimed_or_supported=False)], "/no", None),  # Sarah's
    ([_member("spouse", 44, spouse_is_dependent=False)], "/no", None),  # DeShawn's (3e1.Q1)
    ([_member("spouse", 44, spouse_is_dependent=True)], "/yes", "Spouse"),
    ([_member("daughter", 9)], "/yes", "Daughter"),  # Andrés's
], ids=["no_one", "roommate", "spouse_on_the_tax_return", "spouse_a_dependent", "daughter"])
def test_do_you_have_dependents_answers_yes_only_when_line_2_lists_someone(session, members, ticks, row_a):
    _household(session, members)
    printed = resolve(load_schema("schedule_j"), session)
    assert (printed.get("check2"), printed.get("Dependant Relation 2a")) == (ticks, row_a)

def test_an_unanswered_household_list_refuses_line_2_rather_than_answer_no(session):
    _household(session, None)
    with pytest.raises(IncomeStepGap) as refused:
        resolve(load_schema("schedule_j"), session)
    assert (str(refused.value), refused.value.step) == (HOUSEHOLD_UNANSWERED, "income_info")
```

```python
def test_form_101_without_a_county_names_the_your_information_step(self, api_client, session_with_debtor):
    # A session saved before 3e1 has no county (3e1 spec §9.2).
    household = session_with_debtor.household
    household.county = None
    household.save()
    resp = api_client.post(self.URL, {"session_id": session_with_debtor.id, "form_type": "form_101"}, format="json")
    assert resp.status_code == status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY
    assert resp.data == {"form_type": "form_101", "step": "debtor_info",
                         "error": "Choose your county on the Your Information step."}
    assert not GeneratedForm.objects.filter(session=session_with_debtor).exists()
```

### Task 11: Review shows the household, the fee-waiver page pre-fills 103B line 1's count, and household size leaves the API

Review shows three things: the marriage answer in the form's words, line 13's household count, and the county (§8.3). The fee-waiver page pre-fills its Household Size from 103B line 1's count (§8.4). With that last reader gone, `household_size` and `filing_type` leave the `DebtorInfo` serializer, the TS type and the seed's `debtor` dicts (P6, G6). Their columns stay until step 5.

Lines are at 85dc976. Tasks 4–10 move the frontend and seed lines, so find each by the code quoted.

**Files:**

    - a new section after the Personal Information section, which closes at `:111`.
  - `frontend/src/pages/IntakeWizard.tsx`, the `<ReviewStep` render at `:521-535`.
  - `frontend/src/pages/FeeWaiverPage.tsx:25-30`.
  - `frontend/src/types/api.ts:64-65`: `// Household information` and `household_size: number;`.
  - `frontend/src/constants/copy.ts`.
  - `frontend/src/test/fixtures/session.ts:43`.
  - `frontend/e2e/pages/fee-waiver.page.ts:6` and `:12-13`. The plan adds these (see Step 5).
  - `backend/apps/intake/serializers.py:50-51`.
  - `backend/apps/intake/models.py:77-78`. The comment there says the means test uses `household_size`, which is false since Task 7. The plan adds this fix.
  - `backend/apps/intake/management/commands/seed_demo_data.py`: each persona's `"household_size": …,` and `"filing_type": "individual",`. At 85dc976 these are `:68-69`, `:189-190`, `:269-270`, `:343-344`, `:451-452` and `:521-522`.
- Test:
  - `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/__tests__/ReviewStep.test.tsx`: `baseProps` `:9-17`, plus 2 tests.
  - `frontend/src/pages/__tests__/FeeWaiverPage.test.tsx`: the fixture `:54-73` and the test `:151-168`. The test at `:170-193` reads the same fixture.
  - `frontend/src/pages/__tests__/IntakeWizard.test.tsx`:
    - `completedSession.debtor_info` `:448-449`. The contract puts these in Task 5, but the server sent both fields until now.
    - 1 test, which the plan adds.
  - `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_views.py`: 1 test, which the plan adds.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes:

  - **From Task 7, the API's read-only counts:**

    - `household.family_size` (122A-1 line 13);
    - `household.fee_waiver_household_size` (103B line 1).

    Both come from `HouseholdSerializer` `SerializerMethodField`s. Each is an `int`, or null while `income_step_gaps` finds a gap. Every session read, PATCH reply and `update_step` reply carries them.

  - **From Task 6**, in `constants/formText.ts`:

    - `MARRIAGE_ANSWER_WORDS: Record<MaritalStatus, string>`;
    - `MARRIED_NOT_FILING`.

    In `constants/copy.ts`, `NOT_ANSWERED_YET` ("Not answered yet", P21).

  - **From Task 5:**
    - the wizard's `county: string | null` state;
    - `Household`, with its readonly `family_size` and `fee_waiver_household_size`;
    - `PersonaFeeWaiver.household_size`.
  - **From Tasks 4 and 7:** the seed gives each fee-waiver application its size from `count_fee_waiver_household`, at `seed_demo_data.py:791` (85dc976), and `test_api_readback.py` takes its application's size from the Complete reply's `fee_waiver_household_size` (Task 7). Dropping `"household_size"` from the seed's `debtor` dicts is safe only once neither reads `data["debtor"]["household_size"]`, and Step 7's grep proves it.

- Produces:

  - **`ReviewStep`** gains the props `householdCount: number | null; county: string | null`. The wizard passes `householdCount={session?.household?.family_size ?? null}`, read from the context's session, and `county={county}`.
  - **`copy.ts`**, under a new `// Review Step` banner, gains `REVIEW_HOUSEHOLD_COUNT = 'People in your home, counting you'`, preceded by `// DRAFT: counsel C-3`. It is scored, at grade 7 or below.
  - **Review's new section**, `Your household`. It holds three label/value pairs in the step's existing `review-field` markup:

    - `County` → the county;
    - `Your marriage` → the answer's words;
    - `REVIEW_HOUSEHOLD_COUNT` → the count.

    Each value is `NOT_ANSWERED_YET` when null. The marriage words are `MARRIAGE_ANSWER_WORDS[answer]` for `not_married` and `married_spouse_filing`. For the other two answers they are `` `${MARRIED_NOT_FILING} ${MARRIAGE_ANSWER_WORDS[answer]}` ``, so the sub-answer reads in its sentence.

  - **`FeeWaiverPage`**'s pre-fill reads `session?.household?.fee_waiver_household_size`. A saved application still wins, at `:42-43`.
  - **`DebtorInfoSerializer.Meta.fields`** loses `"household_size"` and `"filing_type"`. **TS `DebtorInfo`** loses `household_size`.

**Contract F6, checked (P10).** No path reaches Review without a session read made after the filer's last save. Since none exists, no test is added for it.

- Go Back and Review's "Go to" only move backward, through `moveToStep` (`IntakeWizard.tsx:343-354`). `ProgressIndicator` has no click handler.
- So a filer reaches step 8 only through Continue (`handleNext`, `:305-339`). It saves first (`saveNow`, `:312`). Then `updateCurrentStep` stores the `update_step` reply (`IntakeContext.tsx:155`), which serializes the whole session (`apps/intake/views.py:214-219`).
- The only other way in is a mount, which calls `loadSession` (`IntakeContext.tsx:116`). That covers a resume and a `?step=review` link.

- **Backend tests.** Every test that sets `household_size=` or `filing_type=` on a `DebtorInfo` model keeps working: the columns stay. No test reads either field through the API (grep at 85dc976).

**Line 30, the counts:**

- `count_family_size`: the filer plus every row that lives with them. It feeds 122A-1 line 13 (`13B`, the `family_size` rule), the means test's median and `household.family_size`. This reading is a draft until counsel rules on C-3.
- `count_exemptions`: the filer plus every row answered Yes to "claimed or supported". It feeds 122A-2 line 5 and the expense calculator, whose fallback to 1 on an error stays until DIG-152.
- `dependents`: Schedule J line 2. That is every non-spouse row claimed or supported, and the spouse's row only when "Is your spouse a dependent of yours?" is Yes.
- `count_fee_waiver_household`: 103B line 1. That is the filer; the spouse once, for `married_spouse_filing` or `married_together`; and the dependents, leaving out a spouse already counted.
- `DebtorInfo.household_size` and `filing_type` stay in their table, unread and off the serializer, until step 5 drops them. `IncomeInfo.number_of_dependents` is gone (migration 0019). Schedule J line 1 (`schedule_j_joint_case`) prints "no" for every session.

**Line 32, the gaps of `income_step_gaps`:**

1. marriage unanswered;
2. "spouse is filing with you";
3. the list unanswered (null, not `[]`);
4. a row with an unanswered question;
5. the list and the marriage answer disagreeing, judged only on answered data;
6. income not entered by source;
7. the spouse's income not entered.

**Line 37, the startup checks:**

- The namespaces are `answer:`, `sofa.` and `household.`.
- A sheet column must bind a `sofa.<collection>[].<attr>` or `household.dependents[].<attr>` list that one of the group's slots binds.
- A column bound by a box that doesn't give every answer its own state (`choices` of `{}`, or a single box's `{"true": …}`) is refused.
- A slot binding no column prints is refused, a box's included.
- A box slot with no binding is refused, because a sheet has no box to tick.
- A sheet cell prints a box slot's answer as `option_text` (`apps/forms/field_index.py`), the words the form prints beside the box it ticks, such as "Yes".
- `household.dependents[].relationship`, `age` and `lives_with_you` resolve line 2's rows in list order:

  - the relationship in `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS`;
  - the age as a whole number;
  - `yes` or `no`;
  - an unanswered value blank.

  Any other `household.` binding raises `ValueError`. Their refusals link to `income_info`.

**Line 40, the blank boxes:**

- Schedule I column 2 and 122A-1 Column B print the spouse's amounts for `married_together` only. They are blank otherwise, and Column B lines 5 and 7 stay blank until 3e2.
- Schedule J line 2 (group `j_line_2`, five rows) is the one production repeat group. Its rows past five print on an attached sheet, numbered "2.{n}".
- Keep the sentence about Form 107's rows and the engine's copies, reworded so that only the copies are left to synthetic tests.

**Line 73, income:**

- `current_monthly_income` works on one person's ten amounts.
- `household_cmi` adds `spouse_monthly_income` when `counted_spouse_income` finds `married_together`. The means test, the expense calculator, the `cmi` rule and `line12b_annualized_cmi` read it.
- A spouse under `married_together` with none of their ten amounts raises `IncomeStepGap` in the spouse's words.
- `SpouseIncome` holds the spouse's ten, encrypted and nullable, with the filer's bounds.
- `marital_status` is encrypted. It takes one of `MARITAL_ANSWERS` or null, and anything else is a 400 (`single`, `married_joint` and `""` included).
- The wizard's step 2 body is as line 68 says. Keep "a blank box as null, never $0 unless typed", for the spouse's boxes too.
- Schedule I `Amount 10 Debtor 1` prints `schedule_i_income_total_debtor1`, and `Amount 10`/`Amount 12` both columns. 122A-1 `Debto1.Quest11` prints `filer_cmi` and `Debto2.Quest11` prints `spouse_cmi`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the household gotcha after line 30**

Title it "**The household list**". It must state:

- `Household` is one-to-one with the session. Its `county` is encrypted and must be one of `ILND_COUNTIES`, or it is a 400. Its `members` are encrypted JSON: null means unanswered, and `[]` means the filer said no one.
- Step 1 sends `{household: {county}}` and step 2 sends `{household: {members}}`. The serializer updates only the keys sent.
- Ids:
  - they come from `next_member_id` and are never reused;
  - a row sent with one of the list's ids replaces the stored row whole;
  - a row with no id, an unknown id or a repeated one gets a new id;
  - the reply lists the rows in the order sent, each with its id.
- One row shape: `id, relationship, age, lives_with_you, claimed_or_supported`, then `spouse_is_dependent` on the spouse's row only, with null for an unanswered question. `HouseholdSerializer.to_representation` returns the stored rows as they are.
- No names are stored.
- A 400 is only for a value no answer can have: an unknown relationship, an age outside 0–120 or not whole, a second spouse row, `spouse_is_dependent` on another row, or a 21st row. A missing answer saves, and Complete names it.
- `household.family_size` and `household.fee_waiver_household_size` are read-only, and null while `income_step_gaps` finds a gap.
- Two backend tests pin the frontend's copies of the server's words. `test_household_frontend_copy.py` pins `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS` to `frontend/src/constants/household.ts`, and `ILND_COUNTIES` to `counties.ts`.
- The forms' own words (line 1, its declaration, the glossary sentence) live in `formText.ts`, which no reading-level test scores. DigniFi's own sentences go in `copy.ts`.
